## Domain 1: Security and Risk Management

### CISSP-D01-Q0001 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Canons of the Code of Ethics  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Two canons of the ISC2 Code of Ethics appear to conflict in a given situation. According to ISC2 guidance, how should the canons be applied to resolve the conflict?

- **A.** Ignore the canons and defer entirely to local law
- **B.** Apply whichever canon best protects the certified member from personal liability
- **C.** Apply the canons in the order they are presented, giving earlier canons precedence over later ones
- **D.** Apply the canon that most benefits the member's employer

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** ISC2 states the canons are listed in order of precedence; when they conflict, the canon appearing first (protecting society and the public good) takes priority over later ones.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: The Code exists to protect society and the profession, not to shield the member from liability.
- D: Employer benefit is not the ordering principle; the public good comes first.
- A: Law and the Code operate together; the Code is a condition of certification and is not discarded.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0002 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Canons of the Code of Ethics  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which of the following is the FIRST canon of the ISC2 Code of Ethics?

- **A.** Act honorably, honestly, justly, responsibly, and legally
- **B.** Advance and protect the profession
- **C.** Protect society, the common good, necessary public trust and confidence, and the infrastructure
- **D.** Provide diligent and competent service to principals

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** The first canon is to protect society, the common good, necessary public trust and confidence, and the infrastructure.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: This is the second canon.
- D: This is the third canon.
- B: This is the fourth canon.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0003 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Professional ethics  _(medium, scenario_based)_

A CISSP discovers that a popular product their employer sells contains a serious, exploitable vulnerability that endangers many customers. Management instructs the CISSP to stay silent to protect quarterly sales. Applying the Code of Ethics, what should most strongly guide the CISSP's response?

- **A.** The duty to provide service to principals means following the employer's instruction without question
- **B.** The member should publicly disclose full exploit code to force a fix
- **C.** The duty to protect society and the public good outweighs the employer's sales interest
- **D.** The member should resign immediately and take no further action

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Protecting society and necessary public trust is the highest-precedence canon and outweighs short-term employer revenue; the member should pursue responsible remediation/disclosure.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Service to principals is a lower canon and never licenses endangering the public.
- D: Resigning abandons the duty to protect society and does nothing to remediate the risk.
- B: Releasing full exploit code recklessly harms customers and violates acting honorably and protecting society.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0004 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Professional ethics  _(hard, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO behaviors are consistent with the canon "Act honorably, honestly, justly, responsibly, and legally"? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Reporting findings truthfully even when they are unflattering to your employer
- **B.** Exaggerating risk findings to win budget for a project you favor
- **C.** Accurately representing your qualifications and the limits of your expertise
- **D.** Withholding a conflict of interest from a client to keep an engagement

**Correct answer:** A, C

**Why correct:** Honest representation of qualifications and truthful reporting both embody honorable, honest, and just conduct.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Exaggerating findings is dishonest and manipulative, violating the canon.
- D: Concealing a conflict of interest is dishonest and unjust.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0005 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Preamble and certification condition  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

According to ISC2, adherence to the Code of Ethics is best described as which of the following?

- **A.** A set of optional suggestions for new professionals
- **B.** A condition of certification that all certified members must follow
- **C.** A guideline that applies only while taking the exam
- **D.** A legal statute enforced by government regulators

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** ISC2 states that strict adherence to the Code is a condition of certification.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: It is mandatory, not optional.
- D: It is a professional code, not government law.
- C: It applies throughout a member's certified career, not only during the exam.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0006 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Professional ethics  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A CISSP is asked to take on consulting work that directly competes with a current client's interests. The most ethically appropriate first step is to:

- **A.** Proceed but keep the two engagements secret from each other
- **B.** Accept the work quietly because more income advances the profession
- **C.** Disclose the potential conflict of interest to the affected parties
- **D.** Decline all future consulting to avoid any appearance of conflict

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Acting honorably and justly requires disclosing conflicts of interest so affected parties can make informed decisions.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Income does not justify concealing a conflict; this violates honest/just conduct.
- D: Blanket avoidance is unnecessary; disclosure and management of the conflict is the correct response.
- A: Secrecy compounds the conflict and is dishonest.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0007 — ISC2 Code of Ethics / Advancing the profession  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which action best fulfills the canon "Advance and protect the profession"?

- **A.** Endorsing colleagues for skills you have not verified they possess
- **B.** Refusing to share any knowledge so competitors cannot benefit
- **C.** Publicly disparaging other certifications to elevate your own
- **D.** Mentoring junior practitioners and discouraging unethical certification practices

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Mentoring and upholding ethical standards strengthens and protects the profession, fulfilling the fourth canon.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Hoarding knowledge does not advance the profession.
- A: Endorsing unverified skills is dishonest and harms the profession's credibility.
- C: Disparaging others is unprofessional and does not protect the profession.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Code of Ethics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0008 — Information Security Concepts / CIA Triad  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Ensuring that data is not disclosed to unauthorized persons or processes is the definition of which security principle?

- **A.** Availability
- **B.** Non-repudiation
- **C.** Integrity
- **D.** Confidentiality

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Confidentiality ensures information is not disclosed to unauthorized parties—preventing people from seeing data they are not allowed to see.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Integrity concerns unauthorized alteration of data, not disclosure.
- A: Availability concerns timely access for authorized users.
- B: Non-repudiation prevents denial of an action's origin.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0009 — Information Security Concepts / CIA Triad  _(medium, scenario_based)_

An attacker modifies the dollar amounts stored in a bank's transaction database so that transfers are recorded incorrectly. Which element of the CIA triad is primarily violated?

- **A.** Confidentiality
- **B.** Authenticity
- **C.** Availability
- **D.** Integrity

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Unauthorized alteration of stored data is an integrity violation—preventing data from being changed by those not allowed to change it.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: No unauthorized disclosure of data is described.
- C: The data remains accessible; availability is not the primary concern.
- B: Authenticity concerns the legitimacy of the source of an action, which is secondary here to the data alteration itself.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0010 — Information Security Concepts / Availability  _(medium, scenario_based)_

A ransomware outbreak encrypts files on production servers, blocking authorized users from accessing them, though the data is never read or altered by the attacker beyond encryption. Which CIA property is most directly impacted?

- **A.** Availability
- **B.** Confidentiality
- **C.** Non-repudiation
- **D.** Integrity

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Blocking authorized access to data degrades availability—ensuring data is usable by authorized parties when needed.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: The scenario specifies the data was not disclosed to unauthorized parties.
- D: While encryption changes the bytes, the defining harm here is denial of access (availability), not silent corruption of legitimate content.
- C: Non-repudiation is unrelated to access denial.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0011 — Information Security Concepts / Non-repudiation  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which security property proves that data or an action originated from a particular subject who cannot later deny it, and is typically accomplished using cryptography?

- **A.** Non-repudiation
- **B.** Redundancy
- **C.** Availability
- **D.** Confidentiality

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Non-repudiation proves the origin of data or actions so the originator cannot dispute it, commonly enforced with cryptographic digital signatures.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Availability concerns access, not proof of origin.
- D: Confidentiality concerns disclosure, not proof of origin.
- B: Redundancy is a reliability technique, not a CIA/authenticity property.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0012 — Information Security Concepts / Authenticity  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A system verifies that a received message genuinely came from the claimed sender and was created by a legitimate source. Which property is being enforced?

- **A.** Confidentiality
- **B.** Anonymity
- **C.** Authenticity
- **D.** Availability

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Authenticity ensures that data or an action came from a legitimate, verified source.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Availability concerns access, not source legitimacy.
- A: Confidentiality concerns disclosure, not source legitimacy.
- B: Anonymity is the opposite goal—hiding source identity.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0013 — Information Security Concepts / CIA Triad trade-offs  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A hospital must keep patient monitoring systems running continuously, even if that means occasionally relaxing strict access prompts during emergencies. This design choice prioritizes which CIA property, and at the potential expense of which other?

- **A.** Prioritizes non-repudiation, potentially at the expense of authenticity
- **B.** Prioritizes availability, potentially at the expense of confidentiality
- **C.** Prioritizes confidentiality, potentially at the expense of integrity
- **D.** Prioritizes integrity, potentially at the expense of availability

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Keeping life-critical systems continuously usable favors availability; relaxing access prompts can weaken confidentiality controls.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: The design relaxes access controls, which reduces confidentiality rather than prioritizing it.
- D: No integrity trade-off is described; the trade is access continuity vs. access restriction.
- A: Neither non-repudiation nor authenticity is the focus of continuous uptime decisions here.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0014 — Information Security Concepts / CIA Triad  _(hard, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO controls primarily support the integrity element of the CIA triad? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Cryptographic hashing of files to detect unauthorized changes
- **B.** Redundant power supplies and failover clustering
- **C.** Full-disk encryption of laptops at rest
- **D.** Digital signatures that detect tampering with a message

**Correct answer:** A, D

**Why correct:** Hashing and digital signatures both detect unauthorized modification, directly supporting integrity.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Redundant power and failover support availability.
- C: Full-disk encryption protects confidentiality of data at rest.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0015 — Information Security Concepts / Core principles  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Beyond confidentiality, integrity, and availability, which two additional properties does the study guide list as core information security concepts?

- **A.** Anonymity and obscurity
- **B.** Profitability and efficiency
- **C.** Redundancy and scalability
- **D.** Authenticity and non-repudiation

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** The guide lists authenticity and non-repudiation alongside the CIA triad as core principles.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Redundancy and scalability are reliability/architecture qualities, not core security principles here.
- A: Anonymity and obscurity are not the listed core principles.
- B: Profitability and efficiency are business goals, not security properties.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0016 — Information Security Concepts / Non-repudiation vs authenticity  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

A digital signature on an email both proves the message came from the sender and prevents the sender from later denying they sent it. The "cannot later deny" aspect specifically provides:

- **A.** Least privilege
- **B.** Confidentiality
- **C.** Non-repudiation
- **D.** Availability

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** The inability of the originator to deny the action is non-repudiation; the proof-of-source aspect is authenticity, and both are delivered by the signature.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Signatures do not, by themselves, hide message contents (confidentiality).
- D: Availability is unrelated to signing.
- A: Least privilege is an access principle, not a property of a signature.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Information Security Concepts*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0017 — Security Governance / Top-down approach  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Why does effective security governance require a top-down approach?

- **A.** Only the most junior employees understand day-to-day security needs
- **B.** Technical staff should define organizational risk appetite without executive input
- **C.** Support and direction for security must originate with senior management and flow down to all personnel
- **D.** Security controls should be selected by end users based on personal preference

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** In a top-down approach, senior management sets direction and support, which then flows to middle management and all other personnel—essential for security to be effective.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Risk appetite is set by senior leadership, not technical staff alone.
- D: Control selection is governed by policy and risk decisions, not user preference.
- A: Governance authority and accountability rest with leadership, not solely junior staff.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0018 — Security Governance / Security planning horizons  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A long-term plan that outlines an organization's security goals and objectives over roughly a five-year horizon and is updated annually is best described as a:

- **A.** Strategic plan
- **B.** Operational plan
- **C.** Incident response runbook
- **D.** Tactical plan

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The strategic plan is the long-term plan outlining goals over a multi-year horizon and is typically reviewed/updated annually.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: A tactical plan is mid-term (about one year) and supports the strategic plan.
- B: An operational plan is short-term and highly detailed.
- C: A runbook is an operational procedure, not a planning horizon.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0019 — Security Governance / Security planning horizons  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which plan is mid-term (approximately one year), designed to meet the security objectives defined in the strategic plan?

- **A.** Strategic plan
- **B.** Operational plan
- **C.** Capital plan
- **D.** Tactical plan

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** The tactical plan is mid-term (about one year) and is designed to achieve the objectives set in the strategic plan.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: The strategic plan is the long-term plan it supports.
- B: The operational plan is short-term and more granular.
- C: A capital plan is a financial/budget plan, not a security planning horizon.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0020 — Security Governance / Aligning security with the organization  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The primary purpose of aligning the security program with organizational goals, culture, and operations is to:

- **A.** Ensure security supports the business objectives rather than obstructing them
- **B.** Allow security to operate independently of business strategy
- **C.** Maximize the number of security controls regardless of business need
- **D.** Shift all risk decisions to the IT help desk

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Security must support the organization's objectives, principles, and strategies; alignment tailors security to business needs so it enables rather than blocks the mission.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: More controls without business justification is not alignment and may impede operations.
- B: Operating independently of strategy is the opposite of alignment.
- D: Risk decisions belong to management with appropriate authority, not the help desk.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0021 — Security Governance / Organizational processes  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A company is acquiring a smaller competitor. From a security governance perspective, what is the most important action regarding the existing security plans?

- **A.** Adopt the acquired company's controls wholesale without assessment
- **B.** Review and update the security plans to account for the acquisition before integration proceeds
- **C.** Delegate the decision to end users of both organizations
- **D.** Freeze all security changes until the next annual strategic review

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Security plans must be reviewed and updated upon purchases, acquisitions, sales, or divestitures, because organizational structure changes affect risk and policy applicability.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Waiting for an annual review leaves the merged risk unmanaged during a high-risk period.
- A: Adopting controls without assessment ignores due diligence and may import unknown risk.
- C: Governance decisions of this magnitude rest with management, not end users.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0022 — Security Governance / Security governance definition  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Security governance is best defined as the practice of:

- **A.** Installing and patching all servers in the environment
- **B.** Performing day-to-day monitoring of firewall logs
- **C.** Ethically aligning business objectives with applicable laws, standards, and regulations
- **D.** Writing the source code for security tools

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Governance defines how to ethically align business objectives to laws, standards, or regulations and sets how management acts in stakeholders' best interest.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Patching is an operational task, not governance.
- D: Coding tools is an engineering activity, not governance.
- B: Log monitoring is an operational function, not governance.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0023 — Security Governance / Strategic vs operational  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A new CISO finds that the security team has detailed daily procedures but no documented multi-year direction tied to business goals. Which planning gap should the CISO address FIRST to enable coherent governance?

- **A.** Replace the ticketing system used by analysts
- **B.** Rewrite each server's operational hardening checklist
- **C.** Schedule more frequent firewall rule reviews
- **D.** Establish a strategic plan that links security to long-term business objectives

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Without a strategic plan, tactical and operational activities lack direction; the strategic layer must be established first so lower layers align to business objectives.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Operational checklists already exist; the gap is strategic direction.
- A: Tooling changes do not resolve the absence of strategic alignment.
- C: More frequent reviews are operational tuning, not the governance gap.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0024 — Security Governance / Planning hierarchy  _(medium, sequence_ordering)_

Order the security plans from the LONGEST time horizon to the SHORTEST. (Provide the option labels in order.)

- **A.** Operational plan
- **B.** Tactical plan
- **C.** Strategic plan

**Correct answer:** C,B,A

**Why correct:** Strategic (long-term, multi-year) is broadest, tactical (about one year) supports it, and operational (short-term, detailed) is the most immediate.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0025 — Security Governance / Management support  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

For a security program to be effective, it must above all be:

- **A.** Supported and sponsored by senior management
- **B.** Built entirely by external consultants
- **C.** Focused only on perimeter firewalls
- **D.** Kept secret from most of the organization

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The guide stresses that security must be supported by senior management to be effective, consistent with the top-down approach.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Consultants can assist, but executive sponsorship is the essential factor.
- D: Awareness across the organization is needed; secrecy undermines effectiveness.
- C: Perimeter-only focus ignores defense in depth and business alignment.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0026 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / CISO  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which executive is responsible for the vision and strategy of the organization's security program?

- **A.** Chief Information Security Officer (CISO)
- **B.** Chief Financial Officer (CFO)
- **C.** Data Custodian
- **D.** Chief Executive Officer (CEO)

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The CISO is responsible for the vision and strategy of the organization's security program.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: The CFO is responsible for organizational finances and assets.
- D: The CEO is responsible for everything relating to the organization overall, not specifically the security program strategy.
- C: A data custodian implements data protection, an operational role.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0027 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / CIO vs CISO  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

How does the responsibility of the CIO differ from that of the CISO?

- **A.** The CIO handles payroll while the CISO handles marketing
- **B.** They are identical roles with different titles
- **C.** The CIO owns the vision/strategy for information systems; the CISO owns the vision/strategy for the security program
- **D.** The CIO owns the security program; the CISO owns physical facilities

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** The CIO is responsible for the vision and strategy of the organization's information systems, while the CISO is responsible for the vision and strategy of its security program.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: The CISO, not the CIO, owns the security program; facilities are not the CISO's defining role.
- B: They are distinct roles with different mandates.
- A: Neither role is defined by payroll or marketing.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0028 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Data Owner  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Who is responsible for the classification and protection of a given set of data?

- **A.** Data Owner
- **B.** Auditor
- **C.** System Administrator
- **D.** Data Custodian

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The data owner is responsible for the classification and protection decisions for their data.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: The custodian implements protection but does not set classification.
- C: The administrator maintains the system, a privileged operational role.
- B: The auditor verifies compliance, not classification ownership.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0029 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Data Custodian  _(medium, scenario_based)_

A data owner classifies a dataset as "Confidential" and specifies it must be encrypted at rest. The team member who actually configures and maintains that encryption on the storage system is acting as the:

- **A.** Data Owner
- **B.** Data Steward
- **C.** Security Manager
- **D.** Data Custodian

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Implementing and maintaining the protection the owner specifies (e.g., encryption) is the custodian's responsibility.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: The owner sets classification and protection requirements but does not necessarily implement them.
- B: The steward provides subject-matter expertise for specific data, not hands-on protection implementation.
- C: The security manager develops and manages the broader security program.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0030 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Data Steward  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which role provides subject-matter expertise for specific data, such as ensuring it is used and interpreted correctly?

- **A.** Data Custodian
- **B.** Data Steward
- **C.** Administrator
- **D.** User

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** The data steward provides subject-matter expertise for specific data.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: The custodian implements protection, not subject-matter expertise.
- C: The administrator maintains the system.
- D: A user simply accesses the system.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0031 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Security roles  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which security role is primarily responsible for detecting, analyzing, and responding to security threats and attacks?

- **A.** Security Analyst
- **B.** Security Engineer
- **C.** Data Owner
- **D.** Security Manager

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The security analyst detects, analyzes, and responds to security threats and attacks.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: The security engineer designs, tests, and implements security solutions.
- D: The security manager develops and manages the security program and its personnel.
- C: The data owner sets classification and protection for data.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0032 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Auditor  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

To preserve independence, the role responsible for auditing compliance with the security policy should NOT also be the person who:

- **A.** Completes annual security training
- **B.** Implements and maintains the information systems being audited
- **C.** Attends company all-hands meetings
- **D.** Reads published security awareness emails

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** The auditor verifies compliance and must remain independent from the administrator who implements/maintains the systems—separating these duties avoids a conflict of interest.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Reading awareness emails does not compromise audit independence.
- C: Attending meetings is unrelated to audit independence.
- A: Completing training does not conflict with the audit role.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0033 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Administrator privilege  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

In the system roles described by the guide, the administrator is best characterized as a:

- **A.** Privileged role responsible for implementing and maintaining the information system
- **B.** Non-privileged subject who only accesses the system
- **C.** Role that sets the organization's risk appetite
- **D.** External regulator who enforces national law

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The administrator is a privileged role responsible for implementing and maintaining the information system.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: That describes a user, not an administrator.
- D: Regulators are external to the system role model.
- C: Risk appetite is set by senior management, not the administrator.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0034 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Boards and committees  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Boards and committees primarily exist within an organization to:

- **A.** Perform daily patching of endpoints
- **B.** Make key organizational decisions and provide oversight
- **C.** Respond to individual help-desk tickets
- **D.** Write application source code

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Boards and committees are groups responsible for making key organizational decisions and providing governance oversight.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Patching is an operational task handled by IT staff.
- D: Coding is an engineering function.
- C: Help-desk response is an operational support task.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0035 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / CEO accountability  _(medium, scenario_based)_

After a major breach, regulators ask who holds ultimate accountability for everything relating to the organization, including its overall risk posture. The most accurate answer is the:

- **A.** Help-desk technician
- **B.** Data Custodian
- **C.** Security Analyst
- **D.** Chief Executive Officer (CEO)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** The CEO is responsible for everything relating to the organization, holding ultimate accountability even when functions are delegated.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: The analyst handles detection and response, not ultimate organizational accountability.
- B: The custodian implements data protection at an operational level.
- A: The technician provides support and has no organizational accountability.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0036 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / CFO scope  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

The Chief Financial Officer (CFO) is responsible for everything relating to:

- **A.** The security program's technical controls
- **B.** Organizational finances and assets
- **C.** Source code repositories
- **D.** Physical badge issuance

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** The CFO is responsible for everything relating to organizational finances and assets.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Security controls fall under the CISO.
- C: Code repositories fall under engineering/IT, not the CFO.
- D: Badge issuance is a physical security operational task.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0037 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Role separation  _(hard, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO statements correctly describe data-related responsibilities? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** The data custodian implements the protection mechanisms for the data
- **B.** The data owner decides the classification level of the data
- **C.** The auditor implements encryption on the storage array
- **D.** The user defines the organization's data retention policy

**Correct answer:** A, B

**Why correct:** Owners set classification; custodians implement protection—the canonical separation between accountability and implementation.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Retention policy is set by owners/management, not end users.
- C: Auditors verify compliance and must stay independent of implementation tasks like configuring encryption.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0038 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / Roles-to-responsibilities mapping  _(hard, matching)_

Match each role (1-4) to its primary responsibility (A-D).

1. Data Owner
2. Data Custodian
3. Auditor
4. Security Engineer

- **A.** Sets data classification and protection requirements
- **B.** Implements and maintains data protection mechanisms
- **C.** Verifies compliance with the security policy
- **D.** Designs, tests, and implements security solutions

**Correct answer:** 1-A,2-B,3-C,4-D

**Why correct:** Data Owner → sets classification/protection (B); Data Custodian → implements protection (A); Auditor → verifies compliance (D); Security Engineer → designs/tests/implements solutions (C).

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0039 — Organizational Roles and Responsibilities / User role  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

In the system role model, a "user" is best defined as:

- **A.** The independent reviewer of policy compliance
- **B.** The person who sets the organization's strategic security vision
- **C.** The privileged maintainer of the system
- **D.** A subject with access to the information system

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A user is a subject with access to the information system.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Strategic vision is the CISO's responsibility.
- C: The privileged maintainer is the administrator.
- A: Independent compliance review is the auditor's role.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Organizational Roles and Responsibilities*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0040 — Security Governance / Stakeholder interest  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

Security governance "sets the stage for how an organization's management will act." Acting in this capacity, management is expected to act primarily in the best interest of:

- **A.** The most technically skilled engineer
- **B.** The business stakeholders
- **C.** The lowest-cost vendor
- **D.** External attackers

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Governance establishes that management acts in the best interest of the business stakeholders.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Individual technical skill does not define whose interests governance serves.
- D: Acting for attackers is nonsensical and contrary to governance.
- C: Cost minimization is not the governing interest; stakeholder interest is.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Aligning Security and the Organization*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0041 — Security Awareness Training / Awareness vs training vs education  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

In the awareness/training/education model, which activity is intended to CHANGE behavior by enhancing the specific skills employees need to follow security policies?

- **A.** Training
- **B.** Education
- **C.** Awareness
- **D.** Certification renewal

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Training focuses on enhancing skills so personnel can adhere to security policies, often via hands-on sessions and role-specific instruction.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Awareness reinforces what to pay attention to (the "what"), not deep skill-building.
- B: Education provides comprehensive knowledge beyond immediate job functions.
- D: Certification renewal is an administrative credential activity, not the behavior-changing skill layer.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Security Awareness Training and Education*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0042 — Security Awareness Training / Awareness  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Posters, emails, and periodic reminders about security protocols are common methods used to deliver which program element?

- **A.** Security awareness
- **B.** Disaster recovery
- **C.** Security education
- **D.** Penetration testing

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Awareness uses methods such as emails, posters, workshops, and regular updates to keep security top-of-mind.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Education uses formal courses, certifications, and advanced programs.
- D: Penetration testing is an assessment activity, not awareness delivery.
- B: Disaster recovery is a continuity activity, not awareness delivery.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Security Awareness Training and Education*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0043 — Security Awareness Training / Program effectiveness  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A CISO wants to evaluate whether the security awareness program is actually reducing risk. Which approach best measures program effectiveness rather than mere completion?

- **A.** Measure how many posters were printed and displayed
- **B.** Count how many employees opened the awareness email
- **C.** Track click rates on simulated phishing campaigns over time and correlate with reported incidents
- **D.** Record the total budget spent on the program

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Evaluating effectiveness requires outcome metrics—e.g., changes in simulated-phishing susceptibility and incident trends—rather than activity counts.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Email opens measure exposure, not behavior change or risk reduction.
- A: Poster counts are an activity input, not an effectiveness outcome.
- D: Spend is a cost input and says nothing about effectiveness.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Security Awareness Training and Education*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0044 — Security Awareness Training / Regulatory-driven training  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Targeted training programs that ensure adherence to standards such as PCI DSS, FISMA, and ISO 27001 are an example of using awareness/training to achieve:

- **A.** Network segmentation
- **B.** Cryptographic key escrow
- **C.** Regulatory compliance
- **D.** Physical perimeter defense

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Training can be designed to achieve regulatory compliance with standards like PCI DSS, FISMA, and ISO 27001.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Segmentation is a network control, not a training outcome.
- B: Key escrow is a cryptographic process.
- D: Perimeter defense is a physical control.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Practical Applications and Scenarios*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0045 — Security Awareness Training / Administrative controls  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Security policies and procedures distributed to employees during onboarding and at regular intervals are an example of which control category?

- **A.** Compensating controls
- **B.** Technical controls
- **C.** Physical controls
- **D.** Administrative controls

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Policies and procedures delivered through onboarding and recurring training are administrative (managerial) controls.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Technical controls are implemented in hardware/software.
- C: Physical controls protect tangible assets and facilities.
- A: Compensating controls substitute for an infeasible primary control; that is not what is described here.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Practical Applications and Scenarios*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0046 — Social Engineering / Definition  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Social engineering is best described as:

- **A.** A method for encrypting data in transit
- **B.** A process for classifying organizational assets
- **C.** A technique for load-balancing network traffic
- **D.** Psychological manipulation that deceives individuals into divulging confidential information

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Social engineering uses psychological manipulation to deceive people into divulging confidential or personal information, often for fraud.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Encryption protects data, not a manipulation technique.
- C: Load balancing is a networking function.
- B: Asset classification is a governance/asset-security activity.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0047 — Social Engineering / Phishing variants  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A finance employee receives a highly customized email that references their manager by name and a real internal project, attempting to trick them into wiring funds. This is best classified as:

- **A.** Vishing
- **B.** Dumpster diving
- **C.** Spear phishing
- **D.** Smishing

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Spear phishing is a targeted phishing attack customized to a specific individual or group using personalized details.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Vishing is voice phishing conducted over the phone.
- D: Smishing is phishing via SMS messages.
- B: Dumpster diving is sifting through trash for information, a physical tactic.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0048 — Social Engineering / Whaling  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A phishing attack that specifically targets a high-value individual such as a CEO or CFO is known as:

- **A.** Spim
- **B.** Whaling
- **C.** Smishing
- **D.** Tailgating

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Whaling is phishing aimed at high-profile, high-value targets such as senior executives.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Smishing uses SMS as the delivery channel.
- A: Spim is spam over instant messaging.
- D: Tailgating is a physical access tactic.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0049 — Social Engineering / Vishing vs smishing  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Phishing conducted through SMS text messages is called:

- **A.** Smishing
- **B.** Whaling
- **C.** Pretexting
- **D.** Vishing

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Smishing is phishing carried out through SMS messages.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Vishing is voice phishing over the phone.
- B: Whaling targets high-value executives.
- C: Pretexting uses a fabricated scenario to obtain information.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0050 — Social Engineering / Pretexting  _(medium, scenario_based)_

An attacker calls the help desk pretending to be a traveling executive locked out of their account, inventing a detailed but false back-story to pressure the agent into a password reset. This technique is:

- **A.** Quid pro quo
- **B.** Piggybacking
- **C.** Pretexting
- **D.** Shoulder surfing

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Pretexting uses a fabricated but believable scenario (a pretext) to manipulate a target into providing access or information.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Quid pro quo offers a benefit in exchange for information or access.
- D: Shoulder surfing is visually observing someone to capture information.
- B: Piggybacking is gaining physical entry by getting someone to hold a door.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0051 — Social Engineering / Quid pro quo  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A caller posing as IT support offers to "fix a slow computer" in exchange for the employee's login credentials. Which technique does this represent?

- **A.** Whaling
- **B.** Smishing
- **C.** Dumpster diving
- **D.** Quid pro quo

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Quid pro quo offers something of value (help/fix) in exchange for information or access.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Whaling targets executives specifically.
- B: Smishing uses SMS.
- C: Dumpster diving searches trash for information.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0052 — Social Engineering / Physical tactics  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

An unauthorized person follows an employee through a secured door WITHOUT the employee's knowledge. This is best described as:

- **A.** Piggybacking
- **B.** Shoulder surfing
- **C.** Vishing
- **D.** Tailgating

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Tailgating is following someone through a secure door without their knowledge or consent.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Piggybacking involves the employee knowingly holding the door (often via an empathy ploy).
- B: Shoulder surfing is observing to capture information such as passwords.
- C: Vishing is voice phishing.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Physical Social Engineering Tactics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0053 — Social Engineering / Physical tactics  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

The KEY difference between piggybacking and tailgating, as taught in the guide, is that piggybacking:

- **A.** Always requires cloning an access badge
- **B.** Is a purely digital, network-based attack
- **C.** Only occurs in fully unmanned facilities
- **D.** Involves the authorized person knowingly granting entry, often after being asked to hold the door

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Piggybacking is gaining access by getting someone to hold the door—i.e., with the authorized person's knowledge/consent—whereas tailgating happens without their knowledge.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Badge cloning is a separate technique and not what defines piggybacking.
- C: It typically occurs precisely where people are present to be manipulated.
- B: Both are physical entry tactics, not network attacks.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Physical Social Engineering Tactics*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0054 — Social Engineering / Defense  _(medium, multiple_correct_answers)_

A receptionist is unsure whether a visitor claiming to be a vendor is legitimate. Which TWO actions align with social-engineering defenses? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Grant access immediately to avoid appearing rude to the vendor
- **B.** Require valid identification from the visitor before granting access
- **C.** Ask the visitor to hold the door for the next person entering
- **D.** Verify the visitor by contacting their associated company or team

**Correct answer:** B, D

**Why correct:** Defenses require valid identification for all personnel and visitors and verification of unknown individuals (e.g., by contacting their associated company or team) before granting access.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Politeness must not override verification; granting access immediately enables impersonation.
- C: Encouraging door-holding facilitates piggybacking and defeats access control.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Defense Against Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0055 — Social Engineering / Defense  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which control is the MOST effective broad defense against social engineering, given that it targets the human element?

- **A.** Ongoing security awareness training that teaches staff to recognize and respond to manipulation
- **B.** Increasing the office temperature
- **C.** Disabling all email attachments permanently
- **D.** A faster internet connection

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Because social engineering exploits people, security awareness training that helps personnel recognize and respond to attempts is the primary defense.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Bandwidth has no bearing on human manipulation.
- C: Blanket attachment blocking is impractical and does not address phone, in-person, or SMS vectors.
- B: Office temperature is irrelevant to social engineering.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Defense Against Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0056 — Social Engineering / Technique-to-definition mapping  _(hard, matching)_

Match each social-engineering technique (1-4) to its description (A-D).

1. Vishing
2. Smishing
3. Shoulder surfing
4. Dumpster diving

- **A.** Observing a person to capture information such as passwords
- **B.** Phishing carried out through SMS messages
- **C.** Voice phishing conducted over the phone
- **D.** Sifting through discarded trash to find sensitive information

**Correct answer:** 1-C,2-B,3-A,4-D

**Why correct:** Vishing → phone voice phishing (B); Smishing → SMS phishing (C); Shoulder surfing → visually observing to capture info (D); Dumpster diving → trash sifting (A).

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Social Engineering*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0057 — Personnel Security / Separation of duties  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which personnel control prevents any single individual from having excessive control over a critical business function?

- **A.** Least privilege
- **B.** Job rotation
- **C.** Separation of duties
- **D.** Need to know

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Separation of duties divides critical functions so no single person can complete a sensitive process alone, reducing fraud and error.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Least privilege limits access to the minimum needed, but does not by itself split a single function across people.
- D: Need to know restricts information access, not control over a whole function.
- B: Job rotation moves staff between roles; it is a related but distinct control.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Key Security Controls for Personnel Management*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0058 — Personnel Security / Least privilege vs need to know  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

An employee has the system rights to open a folder (their role allows it) but should not view a specific file inside it because it is unrelated to their current task. Restricting that specific file most directly enforces:

- **A.** Need to know
- **B.** Separation of duties
- **C.** Mandatory vacation
- **D.** Least privilege

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Need to know restricts access to specific information to only those who require it for their duties, even when broader privileges technically permit access.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Least privilege limits the rights/permissions granted; here the rights exist but the specific information is still not needed.
- B: Separation of duties divides a function among people; it is not about a single file's relevance.
- C: Mandatory vacation is a fraud-detection control, not access scoping.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Key Security Controls for Personnel Management*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0059 — Personnel Security / Least privilege  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Ensuring personnel have only the minimum level of access required to perform their job functions defines:

- **A.** Defense in depth
- **B.** Least privilege
- **C.** Due diligence
- **D.** Separation of duties

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Least privilege grants the minimum access necessary to perform one's duties.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Separation of duties divides critical functions among multiple people.
- A: Defense in depth layers multiple controls.
- C: Due diligence is the ongoing effort to keep controls effective.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Key Security Controls for Personnel Management*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0060 — Personnel Security / Employment lifecycle  _(medium, sequence_ordering)_

Place the following personnel-security activities in the order they normally occur across the employment lifecycle. (Provide the option labels in order.)

- **A.** Offboarding: access revocation and asset return
- **B.** Ongoing access management under least privilege
- **C.** Pre-employment screening and background checks
- **D.** Onboarding security training and policy acknowledgement

**Correct answer:** C,D,B,A

**Why correct:** The lifecycle runs from pre-hire screening, to onboarding, to ongoing management during employment, to offboarding at termination.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Personnel Security Policies*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0061 — Personnel Security / Background checks  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

During hiring, screening and background checks primarily serve to:

- **A.** Verify candidates' suitability and trustworthiness before granting access
- **B.** Replace the need for onboarding training
- **C.** Determine the company's annual marketing budget
- **D.** Eliminate the need for least privilege after hire

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Screening and background checks (criminal history, prior employment, education, etc.) help ensure candidate suitability and trustworthiness.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Screening does not replace onboarding training.
- D: Least privilege still applies after hire regardless of screening.
- C: Budgeting is unrelated to personnel screening.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Hiring Process*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0062 — Personnel Security / NDA  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which agreement binds employees to protect sensitive information both during and after their employment?

- **A.** Non-Compete Agreement
- **B.** Service Level Agreement
- **C.** Non-Disclosure Agreement (NDA)
- **D.** Acceptable Use Policy

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** An NDA binds employees to protect sensitive information during and after their tenure.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: A non-compete restricts joining competitors, not information protection per se.
- D: An AUP defines acceptable use of company resources.
- B: An SLA defines service expectations between parties, often with vendors.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Employee Agreements and Policies*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0063 — Personnel Security / Non-compete  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A clause that prevents an employee from joining competing firms for a specified period after leaving is a:

- **A.** Non-Disclosure Agreement
- **B.** Acceptable Use Policy
- **C.** Memorandum of Understanding
- **D.** Non-Compete Agreement

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A non-compete agreement prevents employees from joining competing firms for a defined post-employment period.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: An NDA protects confidential information, not employment with competitors.
- B: An AUP governs use of company resources.
- C: An MOU is a broad statement of intent between parties.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Employee Agreements and Policies*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0064 — Personnel Security / Acceptable Use Policy  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which document defines the acceptable and secure ways employees may use company resources?

- **A.** Acceptable Use Policy (AUP)
- **B.** Non-Compete Agreement
- **C.** Non-Disclosure Agreement (NDA)
- **D.** Business Impact Analysis

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The AUP defines acceptable and secure ways employees can use company resources.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: An NDA covers confidentiality of information.
- B: A non-compete restricts future competing employment.
- D: A BIA assesses impacts for continuity planning.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Employee Agreements and Policies*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0065 — Personnel Security / Onboarding  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

During onboarding, new hires are granted access to company resources based on which principle?

- **A.** Anonymous access
- **B.** All-access by default
- **C.** Maximum convenience
- **D.** Least privilege

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Onboarding grants access to company resources based on the principle of least privilege.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Convenience-driven over-provisioning increases risk.
- B: Default all-access violates least privilege.
- A: Anonymous access undermines accountability.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Onboarding Process*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0066 — Personnel Security / Offboarding  _(medium, scenario_based)_

An employee is being terminated for cause. From a security standpoint, which action is MOST time-critical during offboarding?

- **A.** Scheduling their next performance review
- **B.** Updating the company org chart graphics
- **C.** Publishing a farewell announcement
- **D.** Promptly revoking the employee's access rights to systems and facilities

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Offboarding must securely terminate access rights (and retrieve assets); prompt access revocation limits the window for retaliatory or unauthorized actions.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: A terminated employee will not receive a future review; this is irrelevant.
- B: Org-chart graphics are administrative and not security-critical.
- C: A farewell announcement is not a security control.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Offboarding Process*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0067 — Personnel Security / Exit interviews  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Exit interviews during offboarding primarily help the organization to:

- **A.** Understand potential security concerns and reinforce confidentiality obligations
- **B.** Assign the employee additional system privileges
- **C.** Negotiate the departing employee's new salary
- **D.** Skip the need for access revocation

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Exit interviews help surface potential security concerns and reinforce confidentiality agreements as the employee departs.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Salary negotiation is irrelevant to a departing employee's security exit.
- B: Offboarding revokes, not expands, privileges.
- D: Access revocation is still required; the interview does not replace it.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Offboarding Process*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0068 — Personnel Security / Onboarding policy acknowledgement  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Requiring new hires to sign documents acknowledging they understand and will comply with security policies is called:

- **A.** Data classification
- **B.** Penetration testing
- **C.** Policy acknowledgement
- **D.** Risk acceptance

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Policy acknowledgement requires new hires to sign agreements confirming understanding of and compliance with security policies.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Risk acceptance is a risk-response decision by management.
- B: Penetration testing is a security assessment.
- A: Data classification is an asset-security activity.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Onboarding Process*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0069 — Personnel Safety and Security / Duress  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which control allows an employee under threat to silently signal for help without alerting the aggressor?

- **A.** A public social media post
- **B.** A louder office paging system
- **C.** A duress code or silent alarm
- **D.** A monthly newsletter

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Duress codes or silent alarms let personnel alert security discreetly without escalating a dangerous situation.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: A loud page would alert the aggressor and escalate danger.
- A: Public posts are slow, visible, and unreliable for emergencies.
- D: Newsletters are not emergency signaling mechanisms.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Personnel Safety and Security*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0070 — Personnel Safety and Security / Travel security  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

To protect organizational resources accessed by traveling employees over untrusted networks, the guide recommends:

- **A.** Disabling all authentication while traveling
- **B.** Emailing passwords to oneself
- **C.** Using VPNs for secure remote access
- **D.** Posting itineraries publicly

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Travel security includes using VPNs for secure remote access to organizational resources.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Disabling authentication removes a critical control.
- B: Emailing passwords exposes credentials.
- D: Public itineraries increase personal and physical risk.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Personnel Safety and Security*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0071 — Personnel Safety and Security / Priority of life safety  _(hard, scenario_based)_

During a building fire, an automated system can either keep electronic doors locked to protect a data center or unlock egress routes to let people exit. Which choice aligns with security best practice?

- **A.** Lock only the exits nearest to executives
- **B.** Keep all doors locked to protect the data center at all costs
- **C.** Unlock egress routes—human life safety takes precedence over asset protection
- **D.** Disable fire alarms to avoid panic

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Human safety is the highest priority; emergency systems should fail to a state that permits safe egress, even at some risk to assets.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Trapping people to protect equipment is never acceptable; life safety comes first.
- A: Selective locking still endangers lives and is discriminatory and unsafe.
- D: Disabling alarms removes life-saving warning and is dangerous.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Personnel Safety and Security*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0072 — Personnel Safety and Security / Emergency communication  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A comprehensive personnel safety program should include emergency communication that:

- **A.** Relies solely on word of mouth between employees
- **B.** Is documented only in the CEO's personal notebook
- **C.** Is tested once at company founding and never again
- **D.** Provides clear protocols, emergency contacts, and procedures for reporting incidents

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Emergency communication requires clear protocols and a plan including emergency contact numbers and reporting procedures.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Word of mouth is unreliable in emergencies.
- B: A single private copy is not accessible when needed.
- C: Plans must be regularly tested and drilled, not set once and forgotten.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Management Concepts > Personnel Safety and Security*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0073 — Due Care and Due Diligence / Definitions  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which statement BEST distinguishes due care from due diligence?

- **A.** Due care is implementing reasonable security measures; due diligence is the ongoing effort to ensure they remain effective
- **B.** Due care is a one-time audit; due diligence is ignoring known risks
- **C.** They are interchangeable terms with no practical difference
- **D.** Due care applies only to vendors; due diligence applies only to employees

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Due care is the responsibility to implement effective security measures; due diligence is the ongoing effort to keep those measures updated and effective.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Due diligence is continuous attention to risk, not ignoring it.
- D: Both concepts apply broadly, not to a single population.
- C: They are distinct: doing the right thing (care) vs. continually verifying it (diligence).

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Due Care and Due Diligence*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0074 — Due Care and Due Diligence / Applied  _(hard, scenario_based)_

An organization deployed strong controls two years ago but never revisited them; a breach exploited a control that had since become outdated. Which failure does this MOST clearly illustrate?

- **A.** Over-investment in awareness training
- **B.** Lack of due care (never implementing any controls)
- **C.** Excessive least privilege
- **D.** Lack of due diligence (ongoing review and updating)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Controls were implemented (due care) but never maintained or reassessed—an absence of due diligence, the ongoing effort to keep controls effective.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Controls were implemented, so due care was at least initially exercised; the gap is in maintenance.
- C: Least privilege did not cause the outdated-control failure.
- A: Awareness training is unrelated to the unmaintained technical control.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Due Care and Due Diligence*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0075 — Due Care and Due Diligence / Governance implications  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Why is understanding due care and due diligence considered crucial for legal and regulatory compliance?

- **A.** They guarantee an organization will never be breached
- **B.** They eliminate the need for any documented policies
- **C.** They apply only to non-regulated industries
- **D.** Failing to exercise them can create legal liability and demonstrate negligence

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Not adhering to due care/due diligence can lead to legal liabilities and is central to corporate governance and compliance frameworks.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Policies remain essential; these concepts complement them.
- A: No practice guarantees breach immunity.
- C: They apply across industries, especially regulated ones.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Due Care and Due Diligence*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0076 — Legal and Regulatory / GLBA  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which US law focuses on the privacy of financial data and prohibits sharing customers' financial information without consent?

- **A.** Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act (GLBA)
- **B.** Computer Fraud and Abuse Act (CFAA)
- **C.** Sarbanes-Oxley Act (SOX)
- **D.** Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA)

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** GLBA focuses on the privacy of financial data and prohibits sharing financial information without customer consent.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: HIPAA protects health-related information.
- C: SOX governs corporate financial disclosure accuracy for investors.
- B: CFAA addresses computer fraud and abuse.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0077 — Legal and Regulatory / HIPAA  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which regulation establishes requirements for protecting health-related information in the United States?

- **A.** PCI DSS
- **B.** GDPR
- **C.** HIPAA
- **D.** GLBA

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** HIPAA establishes requirements for the protection of health-related information.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: GLBA governs financial data privacy.
- A: PCI DSS is a payment-card security standard, not a health regulation.
- B: GDPR is the EU's general data protection regulation.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0078 — Legal and Regulatory / SOX  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Sarbanes-Oxley Act (SOX) primarily protects investors by:

- **A.** Securing payment card transactions
- **B.** Regulating cross-border encryption exports
- **C.** Improving the accuracy and reliability of corporate financial disclosures
- **D.** Protecting genetic information from discrimination

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** SOX protects investors by improving the accuracy and reliability of corporate disclosures.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Payment-card security is PCI DSS.
- D: Genetic-information protection is GINA.
- B: Encryption export is governed by EXIM controls/Wassenaar.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0079 — Legal and Regulatory / PCI DSS nature  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

Which statement about the Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard (PCI DSS) is correct?

- **A.** It is an international treaty governing dual-use goods
- **B.** It is a US federal statute enforced by the Department of Justice
- **C.** It is an EU regulation enforced by data protection authorities
- **D.** It is not a law but a standard enforced through fines or penalties by the payment card companies

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** PCI DSS is an industry standard (not a law) enforced via contractual fines/penalties by the major card brands.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: It is not a federal statute.
- C: It is not an EU regulation; GDPR is.
- A: Dual-use goods are governed by export-control arrangements like Wassenaar, not PCI DSS.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0080 — Legal and Regulatory / GDPR scope  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

GDPR applies to:

- **A.** Only organizations that handle payment card data
- **B.** Only companies physically headquartered within the European Union
- **C.** Only government agencies inside the EU
- **D.** All entities that process the personal data of EU residents, regardless of where the entity is located

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** GDPR applies to all entities that process personal data of EU residents, giving it extraterritorial reach.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Location of the entity does not exempt it if it processes EU residents' data.
- C: It applies broadly to private and public entities, not only EU governments.
- A: GDPR is about personal data generally, not only card data.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR)*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0081 — Legal and Regulatory / GDPR roles  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Under GDPR, the party that determines the purposes and means of processing personal data is the:

- **A.** Processor
- **B.** Data subject
- **C.** Controller
- **D.** Supervisory authority

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** The controller determines the purposes and means of processing personal data; the processor acts on the controller's behalf.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: The processor processes data on behalf of the controller.
- B: The data subject is the individual whose data is processed.
- D: The supervisory authority is the regulator, not the processing decision-maker.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Roles Defined by GDPR*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0082 — Legal and Regulatory / GDPR rights  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A customer asks a company to delete all personal data it holds about them. Which GDPR right are they exercising?

- **A.** Restriction
- **B.** Erasure (right to be forgotten)
- **C.** Rectification
- **D.** Data portability

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** The right to erasure, also called the right to be forgotten, lets subjects request deletion of their personal data.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Rectification is correcting/updating inaccurate data.
- D: Data portability lets subjects reuse data across services.
- A: Restriction limits how data is used, not deletion.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Rights Under GDPR*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0083 — Legal and Regulatory / GDPR rights  _(hard, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO of the following are rights granted to data subjects under GDPR? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** The right to set their employer's security budget
- **B.** The right to data portability across services
- **C.** The right to access their personal data
- **D.** The right to demand the company never be audited

**Correct answer:** B, C

**Why correct:** Access and data portability are explicit GDPR data-subject rights.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: GDPR does not exempt controllers from audits; supervisory authorities can investigate.
- A: Budget-setting is not a GDPR data-subject right.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Rights Under GDPR*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0084 — Legal and Regulatory / FTC Act  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which US law protects consumers against unfair or deceptive acts affecting commerce, including protection of personally identifiable information (PII)?

- **A.** Federal Trade Commission Act (FTC Act)
- **B.** Basel II Accord
- **C.** Economic Espionage Act
- **D.** HITECH

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The FTC Act protects consumers against unfair or deceptive acts affecting commerce and addresses protection of PII.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: HITECH mandates protection of PHI by HIPAA-covered entities.
- B: Basel II is an international banking regulation.
- C: The Economic Espionage Act protects trade secrets.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0085 — Legal and Regulatory / HITECH  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which act mandates protection of Protected Health Information (PHI) by entities covered under HIPAA?

- **A.** HITECH Act
- **B.** SOX
- **C.** PIPEDA
- **D.** GLBA

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** HITECH mandates protection of PHI by entities covered under HIPAA, strengthening HIPAA's requirements.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: GLBA covers financial data.
- B: SOX covers corporate financial disclosures.
- C: PIPEDA is Canadian privacy law.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0086 — Legal and Regulatory / GINA  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

An insurer is accused of denying coverage based on the results of an applicant's genetic test. Which law most directly prohibits this?

- **A.** Computer Fraud and Abuse Act (CFAA)
- **B.** Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act (GLBA)
- **C.** Sarbanes-Oxley Act (SOX)
- **D.** Genetic Information Nondiscrimination Act (GINA)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** GINA prohibits discrimination based on genetic information in health insurance and employment.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: GLBA concerns financial-data privacy.
- C: SOX concerns financial disclosures.
- A: CFAA concerns computer fraud and abuse.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0087 — Legal and Regulatory / CFAA  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

The Computer Fraud and Abuse Act (CFAA) is best described as:

- **A.** A payment-card security standard
- **B.** An international arrangement controlling dual-use exports
- **C.** An EU regulation governing personal data of residents
- **D.** Early major US cybercrime legislation focused on fraud and related activity involving computers

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** The CFAA was an early major US cybercrime law focused on fraud and related activities in connection with computers.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: That describes GDPR.
- A: That describes PCI DSS.
- B: That describes the Wassenaar Arrangement.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Cybercrime and Data Breaches*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0088 — Legal and Regulatory / FISMA  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which act establishes cybersecurity standards and responsibilities for US federal agencies?

- **A.** PCI DSS
- **B.** Federal Information Security Modernization Act (FISMA)
- **C.** GDPR
- **D.** Creative Commons

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** FISMA establishes federal cybersecurity standards and centralizes related responsibilities for federal agencies.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: PCI DSS is a private-sector card standard.
- C: GDPR is an EU regulation.
- D: Creative Commons is a licensing scheme, not a federal security law.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Cybercrime And Data Breaches*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0089 — Legal and Regulatory / Law-to-domain mapping  _(hard, matching)_

Match each regulation (1-4) to the area it primarily governs (A-D).

1. HIPAA
2. GLBA
3. SOX
4. GDPR

- **A.** Personal data of EU residents
- **B.** Protection of health-related information
- **C.** Privacy of financial data at financial institutions
- **D.** Accuracy of corporate financial disclosures for investors

**Correct answer:** 1-B,2-C,3-D,4-A

**Why correct:** HIPAA → health information (B); GLBA → financial-data privacy (A); SOX → corporate financial-disclosure accuracy (D); GDPR → EU residents' personal data (C).

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Important Laws and Regulations*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0090 — Legal and Regulatory / Applicable law selection  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A US-based e-commerce company stores and processes the personal data of customers living in Germany and France. Which regulation most directly governs how it must handle that customer data?

- **A.** HIPAA
- **B.** SOX
- **C.** GLBA
- **D.** GDPR

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Because the company processes personal data of EU residents, GDPR applies regardless of the company's US location.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: HIPAA covers US health information, not general EU customer data.
- B: SOX covers corporate financial disclosures, not customer personal data handling.
- C: GLBA covers US financial-institution data privacy, not this scenario.

*Source: Domain 1 > Security Governance and Compliance > Determine Compliance Requirements*

---

### CISSP-D01-Q0091 — Legal and Regulatory / Applicable law selection  _(medium, scenario_based)_

A retailer that accepts branded credit cards must comply with security requirements for storing and transmitting cardholder data. Which framework applies?

- **A.** FISMA
- **B.** HIPAA
- **C.** PCI DSS
- **D.** GINA

**Correct answer:** C
