- A: Recovery restores operations; it does not delete evidence.

*Source: Domain 7 > Incident Management > Detection and Analysis*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0081 — Business Impact Analysis / MTD  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Maximum Tolerable Downtime (MTD) represents:

- **A.** The time it takes to restore the process
- **B.** The maximum acceptable data loss
- **C.** The longest a critical process can be down before the business is unacceptably impacted
- **D.** The cost of a backup tape

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** MTD is the maximum time a critical asset/process can be unavailable before causing unacceptable business impact.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Time to restore is the Recovery Time Objective (RTO).
- B: Maximum acceptable data loss is the Recovery Point Objective (RPO).
- D: Backup-tape cost is unrelated to MTD.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Measures of Impact*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0082 — Business Impact Analysis / RTO  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Recovery Time Objective (RTO) is:

- **A.** The frequency of full backups
- **B.** The planned/targeted time to restore a critical process after disruption
- **C.** The maximum acceptable amount of data loss
- **D.** The maximum downtime the business can tolerate before impact

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** RTO is the targeted duration to recover/restore a critical process; it should be less than or equal to the MTD.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Acceptable data loss is the RPO.
- D: Maximum tolerable downtime is the MTD.
- A: Backup frequency relates to (but is not) the RPO.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Measures of Impact*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0083 — Business Impact Analysis / RPO  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A database is backed up every 6 hours. After a crash, the most recent backup is used to restore it. The amount of data that may be lost (since the last backup) corresponds to which metric?

- **A.** Mean Time Between Failures (MTBF)
- **B.** Recovery Time Objective (RTO)
- **C.** Recovery Point Objective (RPO)
- **D.** Maximum Tolerable Downtime (MTD)

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** RPO is the maximum acceptable data loss, determined by backup availability/frequency—here up to 6 hours of data since the last backup.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: RTO is the time to restore, not the data-loss amount.
- D: MTD is the maximum tolerable downtime.
- A: MTBF is a reliability metric, not a recovery point.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Measures of Impact*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0084 — Business Impact Analysis / RTO vs MTD  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A process has an MTD of 1 hour, but the current recovery capability has an RTO of 3 hours. What does this indicate?

- **A.** The recovery capability is insufficient; RTO must be reduced to within the MTD
- **B.** The RPO is automatically satisfied
- **C.** The MTD should be ignored
- **D.** Everything is fine because RTO can exceed MTD

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The RTO must be less than or equal to the MTD. An RTO (3h) exceeding the MTD (1h) means recovery is too slow and capabilities must be improved.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: An RTO exceeding the MTD is a gap, not acceptable.
- B: RPO concerns data loss and is a separate metric.
- C: The MTD is the business constraint that drives recovery targets.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Measures of Impact*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0085 — Business Impact Analysis / BIA purpose  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Business Impact Analysis (BIA) primarily aims to:

- **A.** Identify critical business functions and the impact of their disruption to prioritize recovery
- **B.** Configure the corporate firewall
- **C.** Design the office floor plan
- **D.** Encrypt all data at rest

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** The BIA identifies critical business functions (CBFs), evaluates disruption impacts, and determines criticality/tolerance to prioritize recovery and continuity strategies.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Data-at-rest encryption is a separate control.
- B: Firewall configuration is an operations task.
- C: Floor-plan design is unrelated to the BIA.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Impact Analysis*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0086 — Business Impact Analysis / Metric mapping  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

Which set correctly pairs the recovery metrics with what they measure?

- **A.** All three measure the same thing
- **B.** MTD = max data loss; RTO = downtime tolerance; RPO = restore time
- **C.** MTD = restore time; RTO = data loss; RPO = downtime tolerance
- **D.** MTD = max tolerable downtime; RTO = time to restore; RPO = max data loss

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** MTD = maximum tolerable downtime; RTO = targeted time to restore; RPO = maximum acceptable data loss.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: These pairings are scrambled.
- C: These pairings are scrambled.
- A: The three metrics measure distinct things.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Measures of Impact*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0087 — Recovery Sites / Hot site  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

A hot site is characterized by:

- **A.** Pre-configured equipment but requiring data transport (~12 hours)
- **B.** Being a paper-only document review
- **C.** Having no computing equipment at all (cheapest)
- **D.** Being fully equipped with near-real-time data, ready within minutes to a few hours (most expensive)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A hot site is fully equipped with systems, networks, and near-real-time data, enabling recovery within minutes to a few hours—at the highest cost.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: No equipment/cheapest describes a cold site.
- A: Pre-configured but needing data transport (~12h) describes a warm site.
- B: A paper review is a checklist DR test, not a site.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Recovery Sites*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0088 — Recovery Sites / Cold site  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

A cold site is BEST described as:

- **A.** Fully equipped with near-real-time data
- **B.** Minimal infrastructure with no computing equipment—cheapest but longest to activate (days/weeks)
- **C.** Pre-configured equipment ready in about 12 hours
- **D.** A mobile trailer configured as a hot site

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** A cold site provides space/basic infrastructure but no computing equipment, making it the least expensive but slowest to bring online (days to weeks).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Fully equipped/near-real-time is a hot site.
- C: Pre-configured ~12h is a warm site.
- D: A mobile site is typically configured as a warm site.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Recovery Sites*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0089 — Recovery Sites / Warm site  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A warm site differs from a hot site primarily because a warm site:

- **A.** Has near-real-time data ready in minutes
- **B.** Has no equipment whatsoever
- **C.** Has pre-configured equipment but requires data to be transported/loaded (taking ~12 hours)
- **D.** Is only a tabletop exercise

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** A warm site has pre-configured computing equipment and basic infrastructure but needs recent data brought from the primary site, taking around 12 hours to activate.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: No equipment describes a cold site.
- A: Near-real-time data ready in minutes describes a hot site.
- D: A tabletop exercise is a DR test, not a site.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Recovery Sites*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0090 — Recovery Sites / Site selection  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A bank requires its critical systems to resume within an hour of a disaster and cannot tolerate significant data loss. Which recovery site is MOST appropriate despite higher cost?

- **A.** Cold site
- **B.** A reciprocal/shared paper agreement only
- **C.** No recovery site
- **D.** Hot site

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A near-zero RTO/RPO requirement justifies a hot site, which is fully equipped with near-real-time data and recovers within minutes to a few hours.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: A cold site takes days/weeks—far exceeding a one-hour requirement.
- B: A paper agreement alone does not provide rapid technical recovery.
- C: No recovery site cannot meet the stringent requirement.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Recovery Sites*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0091 — Recovery Sites / Site-type mapping  _(hard, matching)_

Match each recovery site (1-3) to its characteristic (A-C).

1. Cold site
2. Warm site
3. Hot site

- **A.** Fully equipped with near-real-time data; ready in minutes to hours; most expensive
- **B.** Pre-configured equipment; needs data transport (~12 hours to activate)
- **C.** No computing equipment; cheapest; days/weeks to activate

**Correct answer:** 1-C,2-B,3-A

**Why correct:** Cold → no equipment/cheapest/slowest (B); Warm → pre-configured, needs data (~12h) (A); Hot → fully equipped/near-real-time/fastest/most expensive (C).

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Recovery Sites*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0092 — Recovery Sites / Shared site  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A shared recovery site arrangement between organizations is typically governed by a:

- **A.** Mutual Assistance Agreement (MAA) / reciprocal agreement
- **B.** Software license (EULA)
- **C.** Master boot record
- **D.** Wireless pre-shared key

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** A shared site is used with one or more organizations under a Mutual Assistance Agreement (MAA)/reciprocal agreement defining responsibilities.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: A wireless PSK is a network secret, not a site agreement.
- B: A EULA governs software use.
- C: An MBR is a disk structure.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Additional Recovery Site Options*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0093 — Recovery Sites / Cloud recovery  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

When using a cloud-managed recovery site, a critical contractual element to define is the:

- **A.** Service Level Agreement (SLA) specifying service levels, performance, and cost
- **B.** Wireless SSID
- **C.** Office seating chart
- **D.** Master boot record configuration

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** A cloud recovery site (typically warm/hot) needs a well-defined SLA covering service levels, performance, and cost to ensure recovery expectations are met.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: MBR configuration is unrelated to cloud recovery SLAs.
- B: An SSID is a wireless network name.
- C: A seating chart is unrelated.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Additional Recovery Site Options*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0094 — Recovery Strategies / Failover cluster  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A failover cluster improves availability by:

- **A.** Blocking unauthorized network ports
- **B.** Encrypting all data on the server
- **C.** Issuing user credentials
- **D.** Automatically shifting workloads to a standby node when the active node fails

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A failover cluster detects node failure and automatically moves the workload to a healthy standby node, minimizing downtime.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Encryption protects confidentiality, not availability via failover.
- A: Port blocking is a firewall function.
- C: Credential issuance is an IAM function.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Server Protection*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0095 — Recovery Strategies / Load balancing  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Load balancing supports availability primarily by:

- **A.** Encrypting traffic between two endpoints
- **B.** Distributing traffic/workload across multiple servers to avoid overload and single points of failure
- **C.** Assigning MAC addresses
- **D.** Permanently deleting old logs

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Load balancing spreads requests across multiple servers, improving performance and resilience by avoiding overload and single points of failure.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Encryption is a confidentiality control, not load distribution.
- D: Log deletion is unrelated to load balancing.
- C: MAC assignment is hardware-based.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Server Protection*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0096 — Recovery Strategies / Fail secure vs fail open  _(hard, scenario_based)_

An electronic door lock loses power. To protect a high-security vault (prioritizing confidentiality/integrity), it should be configured to:

- **A.** Fail open (unlock on failure) regardless of context
- **B.** Fail open to maximize convenience
- **C.** Stay in its last state with no defined behavior
- **D.** Fail secure (remain locked on failure)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** For a high-security area prioritizing confidentiality/integrity, the lock should fail secure (stay locked) on power loss—though life-safety areas may instead require fail-open for egress.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Failing open would expose the vault; it conflicts with the stated priority.
- B: Convenience does not justify exposing a high-security vault.
- C: An undefined failure state is unsafe and unacceptable.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Device Failure and Security*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0097 — Recovery Strategies / Fail open for safety  _(hard, scenario_based)_

During a power failure, an electronically controlled emergency EXIT door in an occupied office should:

- **A.** Disable the fire alarm
- **B.** Fail secure (stay locked) to protect assets, trapping occupants
- **C.** Fail open (unlock) to allow safe egress, prioritizing human life safety
- **D.** Stay locked permanently

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Life safety takes precedence: emergency egress doors should fail open (unlock) so people can exit safely, even at some risk to assets.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Failing secure here would trap occupants—unacceptable for life safety.
- D: Permanent locking endangers occupants during emergencies.
- A: Disabling the fire alarm removes a life-safety warning.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Device Failure and Security*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0098 — Recovery Strategies / Power protection  _(easy, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO are power-protection measures used to maintain availability during outages? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Backup generators for long outages
- **B.** A perimeter firewall
- **C.** Uninterruptible Power Supply (UPS) for short outages
- **D.** A network load balancer

**Correct answer:** A, C

**Why correct:** A UPS bridges short outages and generators sustain long ones—both are power-protection measures.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: A load balancer distributes traffic; it is not a power-protection device.
- B: A firewall filters traffic; it is not a power-protection device.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Power Protection*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0099 — Recovery Strategies / QoS  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Quality of Service (QoS) supports continuity of network communications by:

- **A.** Prioritizing critical traffic to maintain performance during congestion
- **B.** Destroying old backup tapes
- **C.** Physically guarding the data center
- **D.** Encrypting all stored data

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** QoS prioritizes critical/time-sensitive traffic so essential communications maintain acceptable performance even during congestion.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Data-at-rest encryption is a separate control.
- C: Physical guarding is a facility control.
- B: Tape destruction is a sanitization activity.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Network Communications*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0100 — BC vs DR / Distinction  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which statement BEST distinguishes Business Continuity (BC) from Disaster Recovery (DR)?

- **A.** They are identical processes
- **B.** BC restores IT systems; DR keeps the business running
- **C.** BC applies only to wireless networks
- **D.** BC keeps the overall business operating during disruption; DR focuses on restoring IT systems/infrastructure after a disaster

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** BC is the broader effort to keep critical business functions running during/after disruption; DR is the IT-focused subset that restores systems and infrastructure.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: This reverses BC and DR.
- A: They are related but distinct in scope.
- C: BC is not limited to wireless networks.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Continuity vs. Disaster Recovery*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0101 — Disasters / Natural vs man-made  _(medium, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO of the following are MAN-MADE disasters? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Flood
- **B.** Terrorism
- **C.** Earthquake
- **D.** Sabotage

**Correct answer:** B, D

**Why correct:** Man-made disasters include human-caused events such as sabotage and terrorism (and utility attacks); earthquakes and floods are natural disasters.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: An earthquake is a natural disaster.
- A: A flood is a natural disaster.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Disasters*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0102 — Disasters / Site location risk  _(hard, multiple_correct_answers)_

Facility LOCATION determines natural-disaster risk. Which TWO location factors should be assessed during site selection? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Whether the site sits in an earthquake zone
- **B.** The brand of the office coffee machine
- **C.** Whether the site sits in a flood zone
- **D.** The color of the building exterior

**Correct answer:** A, C

**Why correct:** Earthquake-zone and flood-zone exposure are key location-based natural-disaster considerations for site selection.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Building color does not affect disaster risk.
- B: Coffee-machine brand is irrelevant.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Natural Disaster Considerations*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0103 — Disaster Recovery / DRP focus  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

A Disaster Recovery Plan (DRP) is primarily focused on:

- **A.** Designing the company logo
- **B.** Restoring IT infrastructure and systems after a disaster, minimizing downtime and data loss
- **C.** Hiring new employees
- **D.** Marketing the company's products

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** A DRP focuses on restoring IT infrastructure/systems after a disaster to minimize downtime and data loss.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Marketing is unrelated to a DRP.
- C: Hiring is an HR function.
- A: Logo design is unrelated.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Planning*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0104 — Business Continuity / BCP goal  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

The goal of Business Continuity Planning (BCP) is to:

- **A.** Reduce the number of employees
- **B.** Encrypt the company website
- **C.** Increase the speed of the office Wi-Fi
- **D.** Ensure critical business functions can continue during and after a disruption

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** BCP ensures the organization can maintain or quickly resume critical business functions through and after a disruptive event.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Wi-Fi speed is unrelated to BCP goals.
- A: Headcount reduction is not a BCP goal.
- B: Website encryption is a separate control.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Continuity Planning*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0105 — Disaster Recovery / DR testing  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Why is regular testing of the DR plan essential?

- **A.** To replace the need for backups
- **B.** To increase the company's marketing reach
- **C.** To assign blame for past incidents
- **D.** To verify the plan actually works and to find gaps before a real disaster

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** DR testing validates that recovery procedures work as intended and reveals gaps/outdated assumptions before an actual disaster occurs.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Marketing is unrelated to DR testing.
- A: Testing complements, not replaces, backups.
- C: Testing aims at improvement, not blame.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Testing*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0106 — Recovery Strategies / Strategy selection  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A web application must remain available even if a single server fails, with no manual intervention. Which combination BEST achieves this?

- **A.** A cold site activated manually after failure
- **B.** Disabling monitoring to reduce load
- **C.** A single server with a faster CPU
- **D.** A failover cluster with load balancing across redundant servers

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A failover cluster plus load balancing across redundant servers provides automatic, continuous availability if a node fails.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: A single faster server is still a single point of failure.
- A: A manually activated cold site cannot provide seamless, no-intervention availability.
- B: Disabling monitoring reduces visibility and does not improve availability.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Recovery Strategies*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0107 — Business Impact Analysis / Critical business functions  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Identifying Critical Business Functions (CBFs) in a BIA helps the organization to:

- **A.** Prioritize which processes to recover first and allocate continuity resources accordingly
- **B.** Choose the office paint color
- **C.** Decide the company holiday schedule
- **D.** Set the marketing budget

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Identifying CBFs lets the organization prioritize recovery efforts and allocate continuity resources to the most business-critical processes.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Holiday scheduling is unrelated.
- B: Paint color is irrelevant.
- D: Marketing budgets are not a BIA output.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Impact Analysis*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0108 — Disaster Recovery / NIST 800-34  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which NIST publication provides contingency planning guidance for information systems?

- **A.** NIST SP 800-34
- **B.** NIST SP 800-207
- **C.** NIST SP 800-128
- **D.** NIST SP 800-88

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** NIST SP 800-34 is the Contingency Planning Guide for Federal Information Systems, covering BIA and recovery planning.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: NIST SP 800-88 covers media sanitization.
- B: NIST SP 800-207 covers Zero Trust Architecture.
- C: NIST SP 800-128 covers configuration management.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Impact Analysis*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0109 — Recovery Sites / Mobile site  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A mobile recovery site is BEST described as:

- **A.** A paper-based reciprocal agreement
- **B.** A fully online hot site with no physical presence
- **C.** A backup tape stored off-site
- **D.** A self-contained site in a trailer/container (often a warm site) that can be towed where needed

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A mobile site is a self-contained unit in a trailer/container (typically configured as a warm site) that can be transported to a needed location.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: A mobile site is a physical, transportable unit.
- A: A paper agreement is a shared-site arrangement, not a mobile site.
- C: A backup tape is media, not a recovery site.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Additional Recovery Site Options*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0110 — Recovery Strategies / Recovery measures  _(medium, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO are technical strategies that improve recovery/availability of critical servers? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Removing redundant power
- **B.** Load balancing
- **C.** Disabling all backups
- **D.** Failover clustering

**Correct answer:** B, D

**Why correct:** Failover clustering and load balancing both improve availability and resilience of critical servers.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Disabling backups removes the means of recovery.
- A: Removing redundant power reduces resilience.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Recovery Strategies*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0111 — Disasters / Non-disaster  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

In disaster classification, a "non-disaster" refers to:

- **A.** A catastrophic earthquake
- **B.** A minor disruption or device/service failure that does not constitute a full disaster
- **C.** A coordinated terrorist attack
- **D.** A complete loss of the primary facility

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** A non-disaster is a smaller-scale disruption (e.g., a single device/service failure) that is handled without invoking full disaster recovery.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: An earthquake is a natural disaster.
- C: A terrorist attack is a man-made disaster.
- D: Total facility loss is a major disaster, not a non-disaster.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Disasters*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0112 — Business Continuity / Management approval  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

For a business continuity program to be effective, it must have:

- **A.** Senior management approval, support, and resources
- **B.** No documentation, to remain flexible
- **C.** Marketing department ownership only
- **D.** A single person responsible with no backup

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Effective continuity planning requires senior management approval, sponsorship, and resource allocation, plus training and testing.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Continuity plans must be documented to be actionable.
- D: Reliance on one person with no backup is itself a continuity risk.
- C: Continuity is an organization-wide concern, not marketing-only.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Continuity Planning*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0113 — Disaster Recovery / DR process  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

After a disaster is declared, a well-defined DR process primarily provides:

- **A.** A marketing campaign timeline
- **B.** Specific, ordered steps to follow during and after the disaster to restore operations
- **C.** The office snack inventory
- **D.** A list of employee birthdays

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** DR processes define the specific steps organizations follow during and after a disaster to restore operations in a controlled, prioritized way.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Marketing timelines are unrelated to DR processes.
- D: Employee birthdays are irrelevant.
- C: Snack inventory is irrelevant.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Processes*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0114 — Recovery Strategies / Data protection  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Maintaining off-site or redundant copies of data primarily supports which recovery objective?

- **A.** Limiting data loss (improving the achievable RPO)
- **B.** Reducing employee turnover
- **C.** Increasing marketing reach
- **D.** Encrypting the office Wi-Fi

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Frequent, redundant/off-site backups reduce potential data loss, improving the achievable Recovery Point Objective (RPO).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Wi-Fi encryption is a separate control.
- B: Turnover is an HR concern.
- C: Marketing reach is unrelated.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Strategies*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0115 — Business Impact Analysis / BIA steps  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A team beginning a BIA should FIRST:

- **A.** Identify critical business processes and their stakeholders
- **B.** Launch a penetration test
- **C.** Encrypt all employee laptops
- **D.** Purchase a hot site immediately

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** A BIA begins by identifying critical business processes and stakeholders, then evaluates impacts and determines tolerance levels (MTD/RTO/RPO).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Selecting recovery sites comes after understanding criticality and requirements.
- C: Laptop encryption is a control, not the first BIA step.
- B: A penetration test is a separate assessment activity.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Impact Analysis*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0116 — Recovery Strategies / Fail secure definition  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

"Fail secure" means that upon failure, a control defaults to a state that:

- **A.** Maximizes access regardless of security
- **B.** Preserves security (e.g., remains locked/denied)
- **C.** Permanently destroys data
- **D.** Disables all logging

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Fail secure (fail closed) defaults to a protected state on failure—e.g., a lock that stays locked—favoring confidentiality/integrity.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Maximizing access on failure is fail-open.
- C: Failing secure does not destroy data.
- D: Fail-secure does not concern logging.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Device Failure and Security*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0117 — Recovery Sites / Cost vs speed tradeoff  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A small nonprofit with a tight budget can tolerate several days of downtime for its non-critical systems. Which recovery site is the MOST cost-appropriate?

- **A.** Cold site
- **B.** Two hot sites in different regions
- **C.** Fully redundant active-active data centers
- **D.** Hot site

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** When days of downtime are acceptable and budget is tight, a cold site (cheapest, slowest) is the cost-appropriate choice.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: A hot site is the most expensive—overkill for non-critical, downtime-tolerant systems.
- C: Active-active data centers are very costly and unnecessary here.
- B: Two hot sites is the most expensive option, far exceeding the need.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Types of Recovery Sites*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0118 — BC vs DR / Scope  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which is broader in scope?

- **A.** They have identical scope
- **B.** Neither addresses business operations
- **C.** Disaster Recovery (DR), which is broader than BC
- **D.** Business Continuity (BC), which encompasses keeping the whole business running

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** BC is the broader discipline (people, processes, facilities, and IT) of keeping the business running; DR is the narrower IT-restoration component.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: DR is a subset of BC, not broader.
- A: Their scopes differ.
- B: Both address business operations/continuity in different scopes.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Continuity vs. Disaster Recovery*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0119 — Business Impact Analysis / Risk assessment in BIA  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The risk assessment within a BIA evaluates:

- **A.** The office coffee consumption
- **B.** The probability and impact of threat events affecting critical assets
- **C.** The marketing conversion rate
- **D.** The color scheme of dashboards

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** A BIA's risk assessment evaluates the probability and impact of threat events on critical assets/processes (qualitatively and/or quantitatively).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Coffee consumption is irrelevant.
- C: Marketing metrics are unrelated to BIA risk.
- D: Dashboard colors are irrelevant.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Risk Assessment*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0120 — Disaster Recovery / Recovery vs continuity timing  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

Conceptually, disaster recovery activities tend to focus on the ____, while business continuity focuses on the ____.

- **A.** Marketing plan; hiring plan
- **B.** Coffee supply; parking spaces
- **C.** Restoration of IT after the event; keeping critical functions running throughout
- **D.** Wireless SSID; firewall rules

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** DR concentrates on restoring IT systems/infrastructure after a disaster; BC focuses on sustaining critical business functions throughout the disruption.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Marketing/hiring plans are not the BC/DR distinction.
- B: Coffee/parking are irrelevant.
- D: SSIDs/firewall rules are network specifics, not the BC/DR distinction.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Strategies*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0121 — Recovery Strategies / Redundancy concept  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Building redundancy into critical systems primarily protects which security property?

- **A.** Anonymity
- **B.** Confidentiality
- **C.** Non-repudiation
- **D.** Availability

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Redundancy (clusters, redundant power, multiple sites) primarily supports availability by removing single points of failure.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Confidentiality concerns disclosure, not redundancy.
- C: Non-repudiation concerns proof of origin.
- A: Anonymity is unrelated to redundancy.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Server Protection*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0122 — Disaster Recovery / Backup/site coordination  _(hard, scenario_based)_

An organization maintains a warm site but has never tested restoring its backups there. What is the MOST significant risk?

- **A.** Backups may fail to restore at the site when a real disaster strikes
- **B.** The MTD will no longer matter
- **C.** The warm site will become a hot site automatically
- **D.** The RPO will drop to zero on its own

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Untested backups/recovery procedures may not actually restore at the warm site during a real disaster—testing is essential to validate recoverability.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: A warm site does not automatically upgrade to a hot site.
- D: RPO does not improve to zero without changes to backup strategy.
- B: The MTD remains the business constraint regardless.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Data Backup Strategies*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0123 — Business Continuity / Training and exercises  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Training, education, and regular exercises in a continuity program help ensure that:

- **A.** The plan never has to be updated
- **B.** Backups become unnecessary
- **C.** Personnel know their roles and the plan works when actually needed
- **D.** The marketing team meets its quota

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Training and exercises ensure staff understand their continuity roles and validate the plan's effectiveness before a real event.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Plans must be updated as the environment changes.
- B: Backups remain essential.
- D: Marketing quotas are unrelated.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Continuity Planning*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0124 — Disasters / Man-made assessment  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

To assess man-made disaster risk for a facility, the guide suggests reviewing:

- **A.** The building's paint brand
- **B.** The CEO's favorite restaurant
- **C.** Local crime rates and police reports
- **D.** The office plant species

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Crime rates and local police reports help assess man-made risks (e.g., theft, vandalism, sabotage) to a facility.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Paint brand is irrelevant to disaster risk.
- B: Restaurant preferences are irrelevant.
- D: Plant species are irrelevant.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Man-Made Disaster Considerations*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0125 — Business Impact Analysis / Quantitative vs qualitative  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

A BIA risk assessment that ranks impacts using categories like high/medium/low (rather than dollar figures) is:

- **A.** Quantitative assessment
- **B.** A configuration baseline
- **C.** A penetration test
- **D.** Qualitative assessment

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** A qualitative assessment ranks risk using subjective categories (high/medium/low); a quantitative assessment uses numeric/monetary values.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Quantitative assessment uses numeric/monetary values.
- C: A penetration test is an offensive security assessment.
- B: A configuration baseline is a known-good config reference.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Risk Assessment*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0126 — Recovery Strategies / Single point of failure  _(hard, scenario_based)_

An audit finds the entire e-commerce platform depends on one database server with no redundancy. This is BEST described as:

- **A.** An acceptable design requiring no change
- **B.** A single point of failure that should be mitigated with redundancy/clustering
- **C.** A form of separation of duties
- **D.** A confidentiality control

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** A lone, non-redundant critical component is a single point of failure; redundancy (clustering, replication, failover) mitigates the availability risk.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: A critical SPOF is a risk that should be addressed, not accepted as-is.
- D: Redundancy concerns availability, not confidentiality.
- C: Separation of duties is an access/authorization control, unrelated here.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Server Protection*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0127 — Disaster Recovery / Recovery vs restoration  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Disaster recovery strategies include both short-term and long-term techniques because:

- **A.** Recovery should never be planned in advance
- **B.** Short-term and long-term mean exactly the same thing
- **C.** Only long-term recovery matters
- **D.** Immediate measures stabilize operations while longer-term efforts fully restore normal capability

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** DR uses short-term measures to stabilize/resume critical functions quickly and longer-term efforts to fully restore normal operations.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: They address different time horizons of recovery.
- C: Both short- and long-term recovery are important.
- A: Recovery must be planned in advance to be effective.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Strategies*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0128 — Recovery Strategies / UPS role  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

During a brief power flicker, a UPS protects equipment by:

- **A.** Blocking malicious network traffic
- **B.** Issuing digital certificates
- **C.** Providing immediate battery power to keep systems running
- **D.** Encrypting the data on disk

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** A UPS supplies immediate battery-backed power during short outages/fluctuations, preventing abrupt shutdowns.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Disk encryption is a confidentiality control.
- A: Blocking traffic is a firewall/IPS function.
- B: Certificate issuance is a PKI function.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Power Protection*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0129 — Business Continuity / Plan maintenance  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A continuity/DR plan should be reviewed and updated:

- **A.** Never, to maintain consistency
- **B.** Only once, when first written
- **C.** Only after a catastrophic failure
- **D.** Regularly and after significant organizational or environmental changes

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Plans must be reviewed/updated regularly and after major changes (systems, staff, facilities, threats) to remain effective.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: A one-time plan quickly becomes outdated.
- A: Never updating leaves the plan stale and ineffective.
- C: Waiting for a catastrophe to update is reactive and risky.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Business Continuity Planning*

---

### CISSP-D07-Q0130 — Disaster Recovery / Order of restoration  _(hard, scenario_based)_

When restoring systems after a disaster, which should generally be brought back FIRST?

- **A.** Only the cafeteria ordering system
- **B.** The least important systems, alphabetically
- **C.** Whichever system an individual prefers
- **D.** The most critical business functions/systems (per the BIA prioritization)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Restoration should follow BIA-driven prioritization, bringing the most critical business functions/systems back first to minimize business impact.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Alphabetical/least-important ordering ignores business criticality.
- C: Personal preference is not a valid prioritization basis.
- A: Non-critical systems should not take precedence over critical ones.

*Source: Domain 7 > Business Continuity and Disaster Recovery > Disaster Recovery Processes*

---


## Domain 8: Software Development Security

### CISSP-D08-Q0001 — SDLC / Security integration  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A core principle for secure software development is that security and privacy should be:

- **A.** Left entirely to the end users
- **B.** Added only after the software is in production
- **C.** Integrated at every phase of the SDLC, not bolted on at the end
- **D.** Considered only during the disposal phase

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Security and privacy must be built in at every SDLC phase (requirements through disposal); change control is especially critical during O&M.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Bolting on security post-production is costly and leaves gaps.
- D: Security must apply throughout, not only at disposal.
- A: Security is a shared responsibility led by the development organization.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Development Lifecycle*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0002 — SDLC / Phases order  _(hard, sequence_ordering)_

Order the following SDLC phases from earliest to latest. (Provide the option labels in order.)

- **A.** Requirements/Initiation
- **B.** Deployment
- **C.** Testing
- **D.** Development
- **E.** Disposal
- **F.** Design
- **G.** Operations and Maintenance

**Correct answer:** A,F,D,C,B,G,E

**Why correct:** The SDLC proceeds from requirements/initiation, to design, development, testing, deployment, operations & maintenance, and finally disposal.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Key Phases of SDLC*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0003 — SDLC / O&M change control  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

During the Operations and Maintenance (O&M) phase, which control is described as especially critical?

- **A.** Marketing analysis
- **B.** Recruitment
- **C.** Change control
- **D.** Office space planning

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Change control is critical during O&M to ensure updates/patches do not introduce security weaknesses or instability.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Marketing analysis is unrelated to O&M security.
- D: Office planning is a facilities task.
- B: Recruitment is an HR function.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Important Considerations*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0004 — Methodologies / Waterfall  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Waterfall model is characterized by:

- **A.** Continuous, overlapping iterations with no defined phases
- **B.** Delivering working software every two weeks by default
- **C.** A sequential approach where each phase is completed before the next begins (with limited feedback loops)
- **D.** No documentation at any stage

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Waterfall is a sequential model: one phase must finish before the next starts, with only limited feedback (e.g., testing back to a prior step). Its rigidity is a noted drawback.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Continuous overlapping iteration describes Agile, not Waterfall.
- B: Two-week deliveries describe Agile sprints.
- D: Waterfall is documentation-heavy, not documentation-free.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Development Methodologies Part 1*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0005 — Methodologies / Spiral model  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Spiral model is BEST known for:

- **A.** Forbidding any iteration or feedback
- **B.** Emphasizing iterative development with repeated risk analysis in each cycle
- **C.** Being identical to Waterfall
- **D.** Eliminating the need for requirements

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** The Spiral model combines iterative development with explicit, repeated risk analysis at each loop/cycle, making it well-suited to high-risk projects.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: The Spiral model is inherently iterative.
- D: Requirements are still essential in the Spiral model.
- C: It differs from Waterfall by adding iteration and risk focus.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Increment Build Model*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0006 — Methodologies / Cleanroom  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

The Cleanroom development model focuses primarily on:

- **A.** Outsourcing all development overseas
- **B.** Rapidly prototyping with end users in JAD sessions
- **C.** Preventing defects through rigorous design/verification rather than relying on after-the-fact testing
- **D.** Skipping design to start coding immediately

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Cleanroom emphasizes defect prevention and correctness via formal methods and rigorous review, aiming to avoid introducing defects rather than finding them later.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Rapid prototyping with users describes RAD/JAD.
- D: Cleanroom is design-rigorous, not design-skipping.
- A: Outsourcing is unrelated to the Cleanroom approach.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Development Methodologies Part 2*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0007 — Methodologies / JAD  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Joint Application Development (JAD) model is characterized by:

- **A.** Never involving the customer
- **B.** A purely solo developer working in isolation
- **C.** Eliminating all requirements gathering
- **D.** Structured collaborative workshops/sessions involving stakeholders to define requirements

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** JAD uses facilitated, collaborative sessions ("JAD sessions") with stakeholders to rapidly define and refine requirements together.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: JAD is collaborative, not solo.
- A: JAD specifically involves stakeholders/customers.
- C: JAD is a requirements-gathering technique, not its elimination.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Joint Application Development (JAD) Model*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0008 — Methodologies / RAD  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A team needs to quickly produce a working prototype to validate an idea with users, iterating fast. Which methodology is MOST suited?

- **A.** Cleanroom with formal verification only
- **B.** No methodology at all
- **C.** Strict sequential Waterfall
- **D.** Rapid Application Development (RAD)

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** RAD emphasizes rapid prototyping and iteration (often with JAD and CASE tools), ideal for quickly building and validating prototypes.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Waterfall's rigidity is poorly suited to rapid prototyping.
- A: Cleanroom prioritizes formal correctness over rapid prototyping.
- B: Skipping methodology invites disorganized, risky development.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Rapid Application Development (RAD) Model*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0009 — Methodologies / Incremental build  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Incremental Build model develops software by:

- **A.** Delivering the entire system only once at the very end
- **B.** Prohibiting changes between increments
- **C.** Avoiding any system engineering
- **D.** Building it in smaller, manageable increments, learning from each to improve the next

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** The Incremental Build model constructs software in increments, allowing learning from prior increments to improve later ones (it resembles Agile but predates it).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: A single end delivery describes Waterfall, not incremental build.
- C: It enables system engineering during analysis/design.
- B: Incremental development specifically adapts based on prior increments.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Increment Build Model*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0010 — Agile / Core values  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Which statement reflects a core value of the Agile Manifesto?

- **A.** Responding to change over following a rigid plan
- **B.** Comprehensive documentation over working software
- **C.** Processes and tools over individuals and interactions
- **D.** Contract negotiation over customer collaboration

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Agile values responding to change over following a plan (and individuals/interactions, working software, and customer collaboration over their counterparts).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Agile values working software over comprehensive documentation.
- D: Agile values customer collaboration over contract negotiation.
- C: Agile values individuals and interactions over processes and tools.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Agile Development*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0011 — Agile / Security in Agile  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A team is adopting Agile and wants to ensure security keeps pace with rapid iterations. The BEST approach is to:

- **A.** Define security controls upfront and integrate them (and configuration management) into the Agile process
- **B.** Let each developer decide security individually with no standards
- **C.** Defer all security work until a final security phase after release
- **D.** Skip security because Agile moves too fast for it

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Security should be defined upfront and integrated into each iteration (along with configuration management), rather than deferred—keeping security continuous in Agile.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Deferring security to a final phase contradicts Agile's iterative delivery and leaves risk.
- D: Agile and security are compatible; security must be integrated, not skipped.
- B: Ad-hoc per-developer security yields inconsistent, weak controls.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Security in Agile*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0012 — Agile / Scrum roles  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

In Scrum, which role is primarily responsible for facilitating the process and removing impediments for the team?

- **A.** Database Administrator
- **B.** Chief Financial Officer
- **C.** Scrum Master
- **D.** Product Owner

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** The Scrum Master facilitates the Scrum process and removes impediments; the Product Owner manages the backlog/priorities, and the development team builds the product.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: The Product Owner owns the backlog and priorities, not impediment removal/facilitation.
- A: A DBA is not a defined Scrum role.
- B: A CFO is not a Scrum role.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Scrum Methodology in Agile*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0013 — Agile / Sprint retrospective  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Sprint Retrospective in Scrum is used to:

- **A.** Deploy code to production automatically
- **B.** Reflect on the completed sprint and identify process improvements for the next one
- **C.** Demonstrate the product to customers
- **D.** Write the initial requirements

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** The retrospective is a continuous-improvement ceremony where the team reflects on what went well/poorly and adjusts for the next sprint.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Demonstrating the product is the sprint review.
- D: Initial requirements precede sprints (in the backlog).
- A: Automated deployment is a CI/CD activity, not the retrospective.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Sprint Lifecycle and Process*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0014 — Methodologies / Methodology selection  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A project has stable, well-understood, unchanging requirements and strict documentation needs (e.g., a regulated system). Which methodology is MOST appropriate?

- **A.** Waterfall (sequential, well-documented)
- **B.** Ad-hoc development with no plan
- **C.** RAD with constant requirement churn
- **D.** Pure Agile with no fixed requirements

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Waterfall suits projects with stable, well-defined requirements and heavy documentation/regulatory needs, where sequential phases work well.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Agile shines with evolving requirements; here requirements are stable/fixed.
- B: Ad-hoc development lacks the rigor regulated systems require.
- C: RAD's rapid churn fits prototyping, not a stable, documentation-heavy effort.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Choosing a Development Methodology (Review)*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0015 — Agile / Agile principles  _(easy, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO are principles of the Agile Manifesto? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Resist any requirement changes after the start
- **B.** Measure progress only by lines of documentation
- **C.** Welcome changing requirements, even late in development
- **D.** Deliver working software frequently

**Correct answer:** C, D

**Why correct:** Agile principles include delivering working software frequently and welcoming changing requirements; working software (not documentation volume) measures progress.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Agile welcomes requirement changes rather than resisting them.
- B: Working software, not documentation volume, measures progress.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Agile Manifesto - 12 Principles*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0016 — Methodologies / Methodology mapping  _(hard, matching)_

Match each development methodology (1-4) to its defining characteristic (A-D).

1. Waterfall
2. Spiral
3. RAD
4. Cleanroom

- **A.** Rapid prototyping, often with JAD and CASE tools
- **B.** Sequential phases completed one before the next
- **C.** Iterative cycles with repeated risk analysis
- **D.** Defect prevention through rigorous verification

**Correct answer:** 1-B,2-C,3-A,4-D

**Why correct:** Waterfall → sequential phases (B); Spiral → iterative with risk analysis (A); RAD → rapid prototyping with JAD/CASE (D); Cleanroom → defect prevention via verification (C).

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Choosing a Development Methodology (Review)*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0017 — Agile / Agile core values  _(medium, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO are Agile core values (the preferred side of the comparison)? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Contract negotiation (over customer collaboration)
- **B.** Working software (over comprehensive documentation)
- **C.** Individuals and interactions (over processes and tools)
- **D.** Following a fixed plan (over responding to change)

**Correct answer:** B, C

**Why correct:** Agile values individuals/interactions and working software (also customer collaboration and responding to change) over their counterparts.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Agile values responding to change over following a fixed plan.
- A: Agile values customer collaboration over contract negotiation.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Agile Development*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0018 — Methodologies / Waterfall drawback  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

A noted drawback of the Waterfall model is that:

- **A.** It is rigid—changes are difficult and costly once later phases begin
- **B.** It has no defined phases
- **C.** It requires no planning
- **D.** It delivers software too frequently

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Waterfall's sequential rigidity makes accommodating changes late in the project difficult and expensive, a primary drawback.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Waterfall delivers once at the end, not frequently.
- B: Waterfall has clearly defined sequential phases.
- C: Waterfall is planning-heavy, not planning-free.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Development Methodologies Part 1*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0019 — SDLC / Disposal phase  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Security activities during the SDLC disposal phase should ensure that:

- **A.** New features are added before retirement
- **B.** The system runs indefinitely without review
- **C.** Source code is published publicly by default
- **D.** Data is securely sanitized and the system is decommissioned without leaving recoverable sensitive data

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** The disposal phase securely sanitizes/destroys data and decommissions the system so no sensitive data remains recoverable.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Adding features is a development/maintenance activity, not disposal.
- B: Disposal ends the system's life rather than running it forever.
- C: Publishing source by default is not a disposal security goal.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Development Lifecycle*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0020 — Agile / Other Agile methods  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Which of the following is an Agile methodology/variant?

- **A.** Scrum
- **B.** Cleanroom
- **C.** Waterfall
- **D.** JAD

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Scrum is a widely used Agile framework; Waterfall, Cleanroom, and JAD are non-Agile (or pre-Agile) approaches.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Waterfall is a sequential, non-Agile model.
- B: Cleanroom emphasizes formal defect prevention, not Agile iteration.
- D: JAD is a collaborative requirements technique, not an Agile framework.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Other Agile Methodologies*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0021 — SDLC / NIST SDLC guidance  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

NIST publications on the SDLC emphasize that across the lifecycle phases, organizations should:

- **A.** Avoid documenting any phase outputs
- **B.** Integrate security and privacy considerations consistently into each phase
- **C.** Skip testing to accelerate delivery
- **D.** Treat security as optional after deployment

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** NIST SDLC guidance stresses integrating security and privacy at every phase, with consistent processes and documented phase outputs.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Security is not optional; it must be continuous.
- A: Documenting phase outputs is part of sound SDLC practice.
- C: Skipping testing increases the risk of vulnerabilities.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > NIST Special Publications*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0022 — Agile / Welcoming change  _(hard, scenario_based)_

Midway through development, a key customer requirement changes significantly. How does an Agile team ideally respond, per Agile principles?

- **A.** Refuse the change because the plan is fixed
- **B.** Ignore the customer and ship the original scope
- **C.** Welcome the change and incorporate it in an upcoming iteration, even late in development
- **D.** Cancel the project entirely

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Agile welcomes changing requirements—even late—harnessing change for the customer's competitive advantage by incorporating it in an upcoming iteration.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Refusing change contradicts Agile's responding-to-change value.
- D: Canceling overreacts; Agile adapts to change.
- B: Ignoring the customer contradicts customer collaboration.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Agile Manifesto - 12 Principles*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0023 — DevSecOps / Concept  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

DevSecOps differs from DevOps in that DevSecOps:

- **A.** Applies only to hardware manufacturing
- **B.** Removes security entirely to speed delivery
- **C.** Replaces developers with operations staff
- **D.** Integrates security into the DevOps pipeline ("security as code"), shifting security left

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** DevSecOps embeds security throughout the DevOps pipeline ("security as code"), automating security testing and shifting security earlier ("shift left").

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: DevSecOps adds security; it does not remove it.
- A: It applies to software pipelines, not hardware manufacturing.
- C: DevSecOps integrates dev, sec, and ops collaboration—not staff replacement.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > DevSecOps Overview*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0024 — DevOps / Access controls  _(hard, scenario_based)_

In a DevOps environment, developers gradually accumulate pipeline permissions from past projects. Which controls BEST address this?

- **A.** Share one privileged account across the team
- **B.** Enforce least privilege and separation of duties with role-based access to avoid authorization creep
- **C.** Disable all access controls in the pipeline
- **D.** Grant everyone administrator access for convenience

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Least privilege, separation of duties, and clear role-based access controls prevent authorization (privilege) creep in DevOps pipelines.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Universal admin access violates least privilege and expands risk.
- C: Disabling access controls is dangerous.
- A: A shared privileged account destroys accountability.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Security and Access Controls in DevOps*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0025 — CI/CD / Continuous integration  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Continuous Integration (CI) primarily involves:

- **A.** Frequently merging code changes into a shared repository with automated build/test
- **B.** Manually deploying once per year
- **C.** Writing code without version control
- **D.** Avoiding any automated testing

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** CI frequently integrates code into a shared repo, triggering automated builds and tests to catch issues early.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Annual manual deployment is the opposite of continuous practices.
- D: CI relies on automated testing.
- C: CI depends on version control.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > CI/CD Pipeline and Security Integration*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0026 — CI/CD / Continuous deployment  _(hard, single_best_answer)_

How does Continuous Deployment differ from Continuous Delivery?

- **A.** Continuous Deployment automatically releases passing changes to production; Continuous Delivery stops at a deployable artifact awaiting approval
- **B.** They are identical in every way
- **C.** Continuous Deployment never tests code
- **D.** Continuous Delivery automatically deploys to production with no gate

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Continuous Delivery produces a release-ready artifact that requires a manual approval to deploy; Continuous Deployment automatically pushes passing changes all the way to production.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: They differ at the final release gate.
- D: Continuous Delivery typically includes an approval gate before production.
- C: Continuous Deployment relies on automated testing to gate releases.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > CI/CD Pipeline and Security Integration*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0027 — CI/CD / Pipeline security  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Embedding security into a CI/CD pipeline (DevSecOps) is BEST achieved by:

- **A.** Letting only one person see the pipeline
- **B.** Removing automated tests to speed the pipeline
- **C.** Involving security at each pipeline stage with automated security testing
- **D.** Running a single manual security review only after release

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** Security should be involved at every pipeline stage with automated security testing (e.g., SAST/DAST, dependency scanning) so issues are caught continuously.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: A single post-release review misses issues introduced earlier.
- B: Removing tests increases the risk of shipping vulnerabilities.
- A: Restricting visibility to one person harms collaboration and review.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Security in CI/CD Pipelines*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0028 — DevOps / Infrastructure as Code  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Infrastructure as Code (IaC) improves security and consistency by:

- **A.** Defining infrastructure in version-controlled code so it is repeatable and auditable
- **B.** Eliminating the need for access controls
- **C.** Preventing any infrastructure changes
- **D.** Configuring every server manually by hand

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** IaC declares infrastructure in code (version-controlled), enabling consistent, repeatable, auditable, and reviewable provisioning—reducing configuration drift and manual error.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: Manual configuration is error-prone and inconsistent—the problem IaC solves.
- C: IaC manages changes; it does not prevent them.
- B: Access controls still apply to IaC pipelines and infrastructure.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > DevOps and DevSecOps*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0029 — Maturity Models / CMMI levels  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

The Capability Maturity Model Integration (CMMI) defines how many maturity levels?

- **A.** Three
- **B.** Seven
- **C.** Ten
- **D.** Five

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** CMMI defines five maturity levels (typically Initial, Managed, Defined, Quantitatively Managed, and Optimizing), used to assess and improve process maturity.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: CMMI has five, not three, levels.
- B: CMMI has five, not seven, levels.
- C: CMMI has five, not ten, levels.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Capability Maturity Model Integration (CMMI)*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0030 — Maturity Models / IDEAL model  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The IDEAL model's five phases stand for:

- **A.** Identify, Detect, Eradicate, Analyze, Log
- **B.** Initiating, Diagnosing, Establishing, Acting, Learning
- **C.** Initiate, Develop, Encrypt, Audit, Launch
- **D.** Inspect, Design, Execute, Approve, List

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** IDEAL stands for Initiating, Diagnosing, Establishing, Acting, and Learning—a model for guiding process improvement.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: These are not the IDEAL phases.
- C: These are not the IDEAL phases.
- D: These are not the IDEAL phases.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Maturity Models*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0031 — Maturity Models / SAMM  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The OWASP Software Assurance Maturity Model (SAMM) is used to:

- **A.** Analyze and improve an organization's software security posture across the SDLC
- **B.** Encrypt data at rest on servers
- **C.** Route packets between subnets
- **D.** Provide backup power to build servers

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** OWASP SAMM helps organizations assess and improve their software security posture and integrate security strategy throughout the SDLC.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Data-at-rest encryption is a separate control.
- D: Backup power is a UPS/generator function.
- C: Packet routing is a network function.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Assurance Maturity Model (SAM)*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0032 — Maturity Models / Purpose  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Software maturity models are used to:

- **A.** Provide physical security for data centers
- **B.** Assess and improve the effectiveness/consistency of an organization's software development processes
- **C.** Replace the need for any testing
- **D.** Encrypt source code automatically

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Maturity models evaluate how effective and consistent development processes are and provide a path to improve them.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- D: They assess processes; they do not encrypt code.
- C: Testing remains necessary regardless of maturity level.
- A: Physical security is unrelated to software maturity models.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Maturity Models*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0033 — Operations and Maintenance / Patch process order  _(hard, sequence_ordering)_

Order the patch management process steps as described in the guide. (Provide the option labels in order.)

- **A.** Rollout
- **B.** Evaluation
- **C.** Testing
- **D.** Verification
- **E.** Approval

**Correct answer:** B,C,E,A,D

**Why correct:** Patch management proceeds: Evaluation, Testing, Approval, Rollout, and Verification.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Patch Management Process*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0034 — Operations and Maintenance / Regression testing  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

Regression testing during O&M ensures that:

- **A.** The software has the most marketing appeal
- **B.** New patches or changes have not broken previously working functionality
- **C.** The build server has redundant power
- **D.** No documentation is ever produced

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Regression testing verifies that applied changes/patches have not adversely affected existing functionality.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Marketing appeal is unrelated to regression testing.
- D: Documentation is still valuable; regression testing does not preclude it.
- C: Build-server power is unrelated to regression testing.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Regression and Regression Testing*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0035 — Operations and Maintenance / Continuous monitoring  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

During O&M, continuous monitoring helps identify:

- **A.** The cafeteria menu
- **B.** The marketing conversion rate
- **C.** The office holiday schedule
- **D.** Unauthorized patches or backdoors and other emerging security issues

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Continuous monitoring during O&M can detect unauthorized changes (e.g., rogue patches or backdoors) and emerging vulnerabilities.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Holiday schedules are unrelated.
- B: Marketing metrics are unrelated to O&M monitoring.
- A: The cafeteria menu is irrelevant.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Continuous Monitoring in O&M*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0036 — Operations and Maintenance / Patch test before rollout  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A vendor releases an urgent security patch for a production application. Before broad rollout, the team should:

- **A.** Deploy it to production immediately with no testing
- **B.** Test the patch in a non-production environment and obtain approval via change control
- **C.** Ignore it indefinitely
- **D.** Apply it only to the developer's laptop and consider it done

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Patches should be evaluated, tested in non-production, and approved through change control before rollout and verification—balancing security with stability.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Untested production deployment risks outages from a faulty patch.
- C: Ignoring an urgent security patch leaves a known vulnerability exposed.
- D: Patching one laptop does not protect production systems.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Patch Management Process*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0037 — Integrated Product Teams / Purpose  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

An Integrated Product Team (IPT) brings together which kind of membership to improve outcomes?

- **A.** Only the most senior executive, working alone
- **B.** Cross-functional stakeholders (e.g., development, security, operations, business) collaborating on the product
- **C.** Only the marketing department
- **D.** Exclusively external contractors with no internal staff

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** An IPT is a cross-functional team (development, security, operations, business, etc.) that collaborates throughout the product lifecycle to improve quality and alignment.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: A lone executive is not a cross-functional team.
- D: IPTs integrate internal stakeholders, not only external contractors.
- C: IPTs span multiple functions, not marketing alone.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Integrated Product Teams*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0038 — Code Repositories / Purpose  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

A code repository (e.g., using Git) primarily provides:

- **A.** Backup power for build servers
- **B.** Version control, change tracking, and collaboration for source code
- **C.** Wireless encryption
- **D.** Physical access control to the office

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** Code repositories manage code versions, track changes, and enable team collaboration (e.g., Git with GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket).

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: Backup power is a UPS/generator function.
- D: Physical access control is a facility control.
- C: Wireless encryption is WPA2/WPA3.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Code Repositories*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0039 — Code Repositories / Secrets in repos  _(hard, scenario_based)_

A developer is about to commit a file containing API keys and database passwords to a PUBLIC code repository. What is the security concern and best practice?

- **A.** Secrets should always be committed for convenience
- **B.** Public repos automatically encrypt secrets, so it is safe
- **C.** Hardcoding secrets is fine if the repo is popular
- **D.** Secrets must not be stored in (especially public) repos; use secret managers and remove/rotate exposed secrets

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Storing secrets in repositories—especially public ones—exposes them to anyone; secrets should be kept in secret managers, and any exposed secrets must be removed and rotated.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Public repos do not encrypt content; anyone can read committed secrets.
- C: Repo popularity increases, not decreases, exposure risk.
- A: Committing secrets is a serious security mistake.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Security Considerations for Code Repositories*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0040 — Code Repositories / Git operations  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

In Git, which operation uploads local commits to a remote repository?

- **A.** git init
- **B.** git push
- **C.** git ignore
- **D.** git clone

**Correct answer:** B

**Why correct:** git push uploads local commits to the remote repository; git init creates a new repo and git clone copies a remote repo locally.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: git init initializes a new local repository.
- D: git clone copies a remote repository to the local machine.
- C: ".gitignore" excludes files; "git ignore" is not the push command.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Basic Git Operations (Example)*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0041 — DevSecOps / Shift left  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

"Shifting security left" in DevSecOps means:

- **A.** Addressing security earlier in the development lifecycle rather than only at the end
- **B.** Deferring security to post-release
- **C.** Moving servers physically to the left side of the room
- **D.** Removing security testing entirely

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** Shifting left moves security activities earlier (design, coding, build) so issues are found and fixed sooner and more cheaply.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: It is a metaphor about timing, not physical placement.
- B: Shifting left is the opposite of deferring security.
- D: It adds earlier security testing, not removes it.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > DevSecOps Overview*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0042 — Maturity Models / CMM purpose  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

The Capability Maturity Model (CMM/SW-CMM) helps an organization to:

- **A.** Assess the maturity of its software process and chart improvements toward higher levels
- **B.** Provide fire suppression in server rooms
- **C.** Encrypt all production traffic
- **D.** Replace developers with automation entirely

**Correct answer:** A

**Why correct:** CMM evaluates software-process maturity and provides a staged path to improve process capability over time.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- C: Traffic encryption is a separate control.
- D: CMM improves processes; it does not replace developers.
- B: Fire suppression is a physical control.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Maturity Models*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0043 — CI/CD / Pipeline benefits  _(medium, multiple_correct_answers)_

Which TWO are benefits of a well-designed CI/CD pipeline with integrated security? (Select TWO.)

- **A.** Elimination of the need for any version control
- **B.** Earlier detection of vulnerabilities through automated security testing
- **C.** Faster, more consistent and repeatable releases
- **D.** Removal of all human oversight from releases

**Correct answer:** B, C

**Why correct:** CI/CD with integrated security yields faster, repeatable releases and earlier vulnerability detection via automated testing.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: CI/CD depends on version control, not its elimination.
- D: Human oversight/approval gates remain important, especially for production.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Security in CI/CD Pipelines*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0044 — DevOps / DevOps definition  _(easy, single_best_answer)_

DevOps primarily aims to:

- **A.** Separate developers and operations into isolated silos
- **B.** Eliminate the need for testing
- **C.** Unify software development and IT operations to deliver software faster and more reliably
- **D.** Replace all software with hardware

**Correct answer:** C

**Why correct:** DevOps integrates development and operations (culture, practices, automation) to accelerate and stabilize software delivery.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- A: DevOps breaks down silos rather than reinforcing them.
- B: DevOps emphasizes automated testing, not its removal.
- D: DevOps is about software delivery practices, not replacing software with hardware.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > DevOps and DevSecOps*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0045 — Operations and Maintenance / CM in patching  _(medium, single_best_answer)_

Why is configuration management important to patch management during O&M?

- **A.** It encrypts the office Wi-Fi
- **B.** It eliminates the need to ever test patches
- **C.** It allows untracked ad-hoc changes
- **D.** It ensures patches are tracked and applied consistently against known baselines, preventing drift

**Correct answer:** D

**Why correct:** Configuration management tracks system baselines and changes, ensuring patches are applied consistently and that configuration drift/unauthorized changes are detected.

**Why the others are wrong:**
- B: Patches still require testing.
- C: CM exists to control changes, not permit untracked ones.
- A: Wi-Fi encryption is unrelated to CM in patching.

*Source: Domain 8 > Software Development Security > Software Operations And Maintenance*

---

### CISSP-D08-Q0046 — Programming Languages / Compiled vs interpreted  _(medium, single_best_answer)_
